\section{Stable-homotopy objects}
\label{chap:stable-background}

This section defines the stable-homotopy language required by the paper.  Located May, Moss, and Toda inputs are stated later in the external-results chapter rather than treated as definitions.

\subsection{Spectra, triangles, and homology}

\begin{definition}[Point-set prespectra and their stable homotopy sets]
  \label{def:point-set-prespectrum}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.BasedSpace,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.Representative,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.StableRelation,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.StableHomotopy,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sphereLevel,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sphere}
  \leanok
  A prespectrum consists of based spaces $X_n$ with specified continuous
  suspension structure maps. The degree-$d$ stable homotopy set is the
  quotient of based cubical maps $I^{d+n}\to X_n$ by based homotopy and
  stabilization. The point-set sphere is the iterated reduced suspension
  of the based two-point space.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Actual stable homotopy on the ordinary source localization]
  \label{prop:actual-source-homotopy-functor}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableHomotopyFunctor,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceStableHomotopyFunctor,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableHomotopyEquivOfSourceIso}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceStableHomotopyFunctor_fac,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceStableHomotopyFunctor_map,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableEquivalence_iff_source_isIso}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableHomotopy_subsingleton_of_source_iso}
  \uses{def:point-set-prespectrum}
  \leanok
  The actual integer cubical stable quotient descends to a functor
  on the ordinary source localization, agreeing with its specified map
  on every point-set morphism. An actual prespectrum map is a stable
  equivalence exactly when its source localization is invertible.
  Source isomorphisms preserve the actual stable homotopy sets and their
  vanishing.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  The quotient maps satisfy the functor laws on representatives.
  Stable equivalences act bijectively by definition, so the explicit
  localization lift applies. Its factorization equation identifies the
  original maps. Mapping a source isomorphism through this functor gives
  the required equivalence of stable quotients.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Cofinal representatives in the actual stable quotient]
  \label{prop:prespectrum-cofinal-vanishing}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.exists_representative_stage_ge,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableHomotopy_subsingleton_of_eventually_level_homotopy_subsingleton,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableHomotopy_subsingleton_of_eventually_nullhomotopic}
  \uses{def:point-set-prespectrum}
  \leanok
  Every stable class has a representative above any specified stage.
  If $\pi_{d+n}(X_n)$ vanishes for all sufficiently large $n$, the actual
  quotient $\pi_d(X)$ is a singleton. This implication also holds with
  explicit cubical nullhomotopies as its premise.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Stabilize two representatives to a common sufficiently high stage,
  and apply the levelwise homotopy relation there.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Abelian group structure on the original stable cubical quotient]
  \label{prop:actual-stable-abelian-groups}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stabilizeHomotopy,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stabilizeLoop_transAt,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stabilizeHomotopy_one}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stabilizeHomotopy_mul,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableGroupStage,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableGroupDimension}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableLevelGroup,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableLevelBond,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableLevelTransition}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableGroup_degree,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableLevelTransition_self,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableLevelTransition_succ}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableLevelTransition_comp,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableGroupColimit,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.Representative.atStableLevel}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.Representative.groupColimit,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableLevelClass,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.Representative.atStableLevel_congr}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.Representative.atStableLevel_sigma_congr,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.Representative.atStableLevel_next,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.Representative.atStableLevel_homotopy}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.Representative.iterate_next_homotopy,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.Representative.groupColimit_homotopy,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.Representative.groupColimit_next}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableLevelClass_bond,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableLevelClass_transition,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.Representative.atStableLevel_iterate}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.Representative.groupColimit_atStableLevel,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableLevelClass_atStableLevel,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.Representative.atStableLevel_const}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableGroupCode,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableGroupRealize,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableGroupRealize_code}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableGroupCode_levelClass,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableGroupCode_realize,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableHomotopyGroupEquiv}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableHomotopyAddCommGroup,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableHomotopyAddEquiv,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableLevelClass_mul}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableLevelClass_one,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.loopClass_const,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableLevelClass_transAt}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.loopClass_transAt}
  \uses{def:point-set-prespectrum,prop:prespectrum-cofinal-vanishing}
  \leanok
  For every actual prespectrum and integer degree, the originally defined stable cubical quotient carries an abelian group structure. Its zero is the constant loop at any allowable stage. Concatenation along every available cubical coordinate, at every stage, gives its addition. The original quotient is explicitly equivalent to the direct limit of its actual level homotopy groups in a cofinal range of dimensions at least two.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  The actual bonds preserve concatenation in an old coordinate. Iterate their induced group homomorphisms in the cofinal range. Stabilizing a representative a fixed number of times defines a map from the original quotient to this direct limit; realization of the level classes is its inverse. Transfer the abelian operations through this proved equivalence. Stabilization extends the concatenation formula from the cofinal range to every original representative.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual abelian stable homotopy on the ordinary localization]
  \label{prop:actual-source-additive-homotopy}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableHomotopyMap_zero_morphism,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableHomotopyAddFunctor_preservesZeroMorphisms}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceStableHomotopyAddFunctor_preservesZeroMorphisms,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceStableHomotopyEquiv_zero}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.Hom.levelLoop,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.Hom.levelHomotopy,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.Hom.levelLoop_const}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.Hom.levelLoop_transAt,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.Hom.levelHomotopy_mul,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableLevelClass_natural}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.exists_stableLevelClass_pair,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableHomotopyMap_add,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableHomotopyMap_zero}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableHomotopyAddHom,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableHomotopyAddFunctor,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceStableHomotopyAddFunctor}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceStableHomotopyAddFunctor_fac,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceStableHomotopyAddFunctor_map,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceStableHomotopyAddFunctor_forget}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceStableHomotopyAddFunctor_forget_map}
  \uses{prop:actual-stable-abelian-groups,prop:actual-source-homotopy-functor,prop:ordinary-source-zero-object,prop:ordinary-source-sphere-representability}
  \leanok
  Every actual prespectrum map induces the original stable map as an additive homomorphism. The resulting abelian-group-valued functor descends to the specified ordinary localization. Forgetting its abelian operations recovers the original set-valued functor on all localized morphisms, including the formal inverses. These functors preserve the previously constructed categorical zero morphisms, and ordinary source sphere representability sends the stable group zero to the categorical zero.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Postcomposition preserves actual cubical concatenation. Move any two stable classes to a common cofinal stage to prove additivity. A stable equivalence induces bijective group homomorphisms, so apply the actual localization construction. Uniqueness of the localization lift proves equality with the original underlying functor.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[From actual level homotopy maps to stable maps]
  \label{prop:actual-levelwise-stable-equivalences}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.Hom.levelHomotopy_stabilize,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.Hom.stableLevelTransition_natural,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableHomotopyMap_injective_of_levelHomotopy_injective}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableHomotopyMap_surjective_of_levelHomotopy_surjective,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableEquivalence_of_levelHomotopy_bijective}
  \uses{prop:actual-stable-abelian-groups,prop:actual-source-additive-homotopy}
  \leanok
  An actual prespectrum map that is injective, respectively surjective, on all level homotopy sets is injective, respectively surjective, on the original stable homotopy groups. In particular a levelwise weak equivalence is an actual stable equivalence.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  The strict structure-map equations give naturality on the cofinal level-group system. Lift a representative for surjectivity. For injectivity, put two classes in a common stage, move their image equality to a later stage, and apply levelwise injectivity there.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual pointwise products and their stable abelian groups]
  \label{prop:actual-prespectrum-finite-products}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.piPrespectrum,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.piProjection,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.piLift}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.piMap,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.piFan,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.piLoop}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.levelPiComparison,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.piLift_projection,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.piHom_ext}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.piFan_isLimit,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.piLoop_homotopic,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.levelPiComparison_injective}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.levelPiComparison_surjective,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.levelPiComparison_bijective,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.levelPiComparison_stabilize}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableLevelPiComparison_transition,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stablePiComparison,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stablePiColimitMap}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stablePiColimitCode,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stablePiComparison_add,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stablePiComparison_zero}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stablePiColimitMap_bijective,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stablePiColimitCode_bijective,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stablePiColimitCode_component}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stablePiComparison_bijective,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stablePiAddEquiv}
  \uses{prop:actual-stable-abelian-groups,prop:actual-source-additive-homotopy,prop:sequential-colimit-finite-products}
  \leanok
  The pointwise product of a small family of actual prespectra is a categorical product before localization. For a finite family, its original integer stable homotopy groups are the products of the original stable homotopy groups of the factors, with the actual projections and abelian operations.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Form the structure maps and based homotopies coordinatewise. Level homotopy groups commute with products. Compare the cofinal level-group systems, use the finite sequential-colimit product theorem, and identify this comparison with the original stable quotient projections. Their already proved additivity gives the abelian group equivalence.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Finite products in the ordinary stable localization]
  \label{prop:ordinary-source-finite-products}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stablePiComparison_natural,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.piMap_stableEquivalence,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableEquivalences_eq_inverseImage_isomorphisms}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableEquivalences_respectsIso,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableEquivalences_containsIdentities,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.prespectrumHasProductsOfShape}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.prespectrumHasFiniteProducts,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableEquivalences_stableUnderFiniteProducts,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceHasFiniteProducts}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceLocalization_preservesFiniteProducts,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourcePiFan_isLimit}
  \uses{prop:actual-prespectrum-finite-products,prop:actual-source-homotopy-functor}
  \leanok
  Finite pointwise products preserve the actual stable equivalences. The ordinary source localization has finite products, and localizing the displayed pointwise product cone gives its limit cone.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Naturality and the proved stable product bijections detect a product of stable equivalences. Transfer this statement to arbitrary product cones by their unique isomorphisms. Apply the localization theorem for finite products to the actual stable equivalence class.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual finite subcomplex approximation of CW spaces]
  \label{prop:cw-finite-subcomplex-approximation}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.cw_compact_subset_finite_closedCells,KIP126.Topology.cw_closedCell_subset_finite_saturated_cells,KIP126.Topology.cw_compact_subset_finite_subcomplex}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.continuousMap_factor_finite_subcomplex,KIP126.Topology.cw_finite_isCompact,KIP126.Topology.cw_finite_subcomplex_directed}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.cw_isClosed_iff_finite_subcomplex}
  \leanok
  A compact subset of a Hausdorff CW complex lies in an actual
  finite subcomplex, with inherited characteristic maps. Every map
  from a compact space factors through such a subcomplex. Finite
  subcomplexes are compact and form a directed family, and their
  restrictions detect the CW topology.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  The weak topology makes a set selecting points from infinitely
  many open cells closed and discrete, contradicting compactness.
  Close the resulting finite collection of cells under their finite
  lower-dimensional boundaries. Finite unions and the weak topology
  give directedness and the closed-set criterion.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Finite support for actual loops and free prespectrum maps]
  \label{prop:free-prespectrum-finite-support}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.BasedSpace.subspace,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.BasedSpace.subspaceInclusion,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.freeMap}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.BasedSpace.map_factor_finite_subcomplex,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.BasedSpace.genLoop_factor_finite_subcomplex,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.BasedSpace.genLoop_homotopy_factor_finite_subcomplex}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.free_level,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.freeEvaluation_natural,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.freeEvaluation_freeMap_comp}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.free_map_factor_finite_subcomplex}
  \uses{prop:cw-finite-subcomplex-approximation,def:point-set-prespectrum}
  \leanok
  A based compact map into a Hausdorff CW space factors through
  a finite subcomplex containing the basepoint. A cubical loop and an
  entire based homotopy have such finite support. For a compact based
  space $A$, an actual map $F_nA\to X$ into a prespectrum with CW
  $n$th level factors strictly through $F_nE$, for a finite
  subcomplex $E\subset X_n$ containing the basepoint.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Apply compact finite support to the based image, including the
  basepoint and, for homotopies, the whole parameterized image.
  The actual free/evaluation equivalence and its proved naturality
  turn the level factorization into the strict prespectrum equation.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[The geometric simplex and its boundary as a ball pair]
  \label{prop:geometric-simplex-closed-ball}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.Simplex.coordinateSimplex,KIP126.Topology.Simplex.coordinateHomeomorph,KIP126.Topology.Simplex.coordinateSimplex_isClosed}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.Simplex.coordinateSimplex_convex,KIP126.Topology.Simplex.coordinateSimplex_isBounded,KIP126.Topology.Simplex.coordinateSimplex_interior}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.Simplex.coordinateSimplex_interior_nonempty,KIP126.Topology.Simplex.coordinateHomeomorph_mem_interior,KIP126.Topology.Simplex.coordinateHomeomorph_mem_frontier}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.Simplex.exists_stdSimplex_closedBall_homeomorph}
  \leanok
  For every $n\geq0$, the actual standard geometric simplex
  $\Delta^n$ is homeomorphic to the closed unit ball in
  $\mathbb R^n$. A point has a zero barycentric coordinate exactly
  when its image has norm one. Thus the same homeomorphism identifies
  the simplex boundary with the boundary sphere, including dimension
  zero.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Eliminate the zeroth coordinate to obtain the convex bounded
  region $x_i\geq0$, $\sum x_i\leq1$ with nonempty interior.
  Its interior has strict inequalities, so its frontier is exactly
  the zero-barycentric-coordinate locus. Gauge rescaling sends this
  region and its frontier to the closed unit ball and sphere.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Homotopy extension across a ball boundary]
  \label{prop:ball-boundary-homotopy-extension}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.Ball.cylinderScale,KIP126.Topology.Ball.cylinderRetraction,KIP126.Topology.Ball.cylinderScale_pos}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.Ball.cylinderScale_continuous,KIP126.Topology.Ball.cylinderRetraction_continuous,KIP126.Topology.Ball.cylinderRetraction_bottom}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.Ball.cylinderRetraction_side,KIP126.Topology.Ball.cylinderRetraction_range,KIP126.Topology.Ball.boundary_homotopy_extension}
  \leanok
  The boundary inclusion of the closed unit ball in any real normed space has the actual homotopy extension property, for every target topological space.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  For $(x,t)$ in the ball cylinder, set $m=\max((2-t)/2,\|x\|)$. The continuous map $(x,t)\mapsto(m^{-1}x,2-(2-t)/m)$ retracts the cylinder onto its bottom and side, fixing each. Closed pasting of the prescribed maps followed by this retraction supplies the extension.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Low-dimensional topology of the point-set spheres]
  \label{prop:point-set-sphere-low-connectivity}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.BasedSpace.suspension_joined_point,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.BasedSpace.suspension_pathConnected,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.BasedSpace.genLoop_eq_const_of_discrete,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sphereLevel_pi0_subsingleton,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sphereLevel_zeroLoop_nullhomotopic,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sphere_zeroLoopClass_eq_const,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sphereLevel_zero_genLoop_eq_const}
  \uses{def:point-set-prespectrum,prop:prespectrum-cofinal-vanishing}
  \leanok
  Every reduced suspension is path-connected, so every positive sphere
  level has trivial $\pi_0$. Positive-dimensional cubical based loops in
  the discrete $S^0$ level are constant. These low-dimensional statements
  do not yet prove $\pi_r(S^n)=0$ throughout $r<n$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  For a suspension representative $[(x,t)]$, the path
  $s\mapsto[(x,st)]$ starts at the collapsed end. A connected cube maps
  constantly into a discrete space, and its boundary fixes the value.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[A closed collapse as one-point compactification]
  \label{prop:closed-collapse-one-point}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.exists_collapse_homeomorph_onePoint}
  \leanok
  Collapsing a nonempty closed subset $A$ of a compact Hausdorff space
  $X$ gives the one-point compactification of $X\setminus A$. The
  homeomorphism sends the collapsed class to infinity and every
  complementary point to its canonical inclusion.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Define the collapse map to the one-point compactification. Its open
  preimages are open either in the complement or as complements of
  compact subsets. It induces a continuous bijection from the compact
  quotient to a Hausdorff space, hence a homeomorphism.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[A boundary-preserving quarter rotation]
  \label{prop:cube-quarter-rotation}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.Cube.squareRotation,KIP126.Topology.Cube.continuous_squareRotation,KIP126.Topology.Cube.squareRotation_zero,KIP126.Topology.Cube.squareRotation_one,KIP126.Topology.Cube.squareRotation_preserves_boundary,KIP126.Topology.Cube.exists_square_quarter_rotation}
  \leanok
  The identity of $I^2$ is homotopic to $(x,y)\mapsto(y,1-x)$ by
  a homotopy preserving the boundary as a set at every time.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Center the square at zero. Interpolate linearly between the identity
  and clockwise quarter rotation, and normalize radially in the maximum
  norm. The interpolation is nonsingular; normalization preserves the
  original norm and is continuous also at the center.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Geometric recognition of the actual sphere levels]
  \label{prop:point-set-sphere-geometric-recognition}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.BasedSpace.intervalInteriorHomeomorphReal,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.BasedSpace.onePointComplementHomeomorph,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.BasedSpace.suspensionComplementHomeomorph}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.exists_sphereLevel_homeomorph_onePoint,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.exists_sphereLevel_homeomorph_euclideanSphere}
  \uses{def:point-set-prespectrum,prop:closed-collapse-one-point}
  \leanok
  The actual level $S_n$ of the point-set sphere is homeomorphic to
  the one-point compactification of $\mathbb R^n$, preserving the
  basepoint, and hence to the Euclidean unit sphere $S^n$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Induct on reduced suspensions. The complement of the collapsed
  cylinder is $(S_n\setminus\{*\})\times(0,1)$, homeomorphic to
  $\mathbb R^{n+1}$. Apply the closed-collapse theorem and the
  stereographic recognition of one-point compactification.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Based high connectivity of Euclidean spheres]
  \label{prop:euclidean-sphere-high-connectivity}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.Cube.collarCoordinate,KIP126.Topology.Cube.collar,KIP126.Topology.Cube.collarHomotopy,KIP126.Topology.Cube.exists_collared_loop_homotopic}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.Sphere.exists_sphere_point_outside_image,KIP126.Topology.Sphere.genLoop_nullhomotopic_of_avoids_point,KIP126.Topology.Sphere.genLoop_nullhomotopic_of_smooth_extension,KIP126.Topology.Sphere.exists_homotopic_genLoop_smooth_extension,KIP126.Topology.Sphere.genLoop_nullhomotopic_of_lt_dimension}
  \leanok
  For any basepoint on a Euclidean sphere $S^n$ and $r<n$, every
  based cubical $r$-loop has a homotopy to the constant loop that
  fixes its boundary throughout.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  An explicit collar makes the loop constant near its boundary.
  Relative smooth approximation preserves that boundary; normalization
  gives a close sphere-valued map and a based homotopy to it.
  The Hausdorff dimension bound makes its image miss a point of the
  sphere. Stereographic projection from that point and linear contraction
  to the basepoint provide the required nullhomotopy.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Cubical loops as actual sphere maps]
  \label{prop:cubical-sphere-representatives}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sphereCube,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sphereLevel_eq_point_or_sphereCube,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sphereLevel_map_ext}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.genLoop_sphereLevel_map_exists_unique,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.genLoop_homotopic_iff_sphereLevel_maps_homotopic,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sphereLevel_compactSpace}
  \uses{def:point-set-prespectrum}
  \leanok
  The canonical cube in the actual iterated-suspension sphere
  $S^r$ identifies based cubical $r$-loops in $Y$ with unique based
  continuous maps $S^r\to Y$. Under this identification, based cubical
  homotopy is exactly based sphere-map homotopy. These actual sphere
  levels are compact.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Induct on the specified reduced-suspension quotient, currying
  one cube coordinate into a based path. The quotient universal
  property gives existence and uniqueness, and the same argument
  with a homotopy parameter gives the homotopy comparison.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Stable representatives through free spheres and finite CW models]
  \label{prop:actual-free-sphere-stable-representatives}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.freeSphereLoop,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.freeSphereGenerator,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.BasedSpace.genLoop_factor_finite_model_of_hasCWType}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.genLoop_freeSphere_map_exists_unique,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableHomotopy_represented_by_freeSphere,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableHomotopy_represented_by_finite_CW_model}
  \uses{prop:cubical-sphere-representatives,prop:free-prespectrum-finite-support}
  \leanok
  Every class of the actual degree-$d$ stable quotient is the image
  of the canonical generator under an actual map $F_nS^r\to X$,
  where $r=d+n$. If the levels have based CW homotopy type, that
  representative factors through $F_nE$ for an actual finite CW
  subcomplex $E$ of a based CW model of $X_n$. The finite model may
  depend on the class.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Apply the proved cube-to-sphere construction and the specified
  free/evaluation equivalence to a representative. For finite support,
  move its loop into the actual based CW model, take a finite
  subcomplex, and use the based homotopy back to the original loop.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Based homotopies give equal actual source maps]
  \label{prop:source-based-homotopy-invariance}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.pathPrespectrum,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.pathConstant,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.pathEvaluation}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.pathConstant_stableEquivalence,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.source_pathEvaluation_eq,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.source_free_maps_eq_of_based_homotopy}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.source_freeSphere_maps_eq_of_genLoop_homotopy}
  \uses{prop:actual-free-sphere-stable-representatives}
  \leanok
  The actual path prespectrum is stably equivalent to the original prespectrum by constant paths. Its evaluations become equal in the ordinary stable localization. Thus based-homotopic free maps, including maps from free spheres with homotopic cubical generators, are equal there.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Contract the path coordinate on cubical loops, then cancel the inverted constant-path map. The free/evaluation equivalence packages a based homotopy as a strict map to the path prespectrum.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual tail truncation and desuspension preserve stable equivalences]
  \label{prop:source-tail-stable-equivalences}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.levelTail,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.paddedTailInclusion,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.desuspensionMap}

  \uses{prop:prespectrum-cofinal-vanishing,prop:source-suspension-desuspension}
  \leanok
  Replacing the zeroth level by a point and retaining the positive tail is an actual stable equivalence. The padded desuspension has natural degree-shift bijections on actual cubical stable groups and preserves stable equivalences of actual prespectrum maps.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Every stable class and relation can be moved to a positive stage. The explicit shifted representative construction gives the bijections and their naturality.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual free-spectrum stabilization and its generator]
  \label{prop:free-sphere-stabilization}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.freeStabilization,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.freeStabilization_stableEquivalence,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.free_level_next}

  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.freeStabilization_freeSphereLoop}
  \uses{prop:source-tail-stable-equivalences,prop:cubical-sphere-representatives}
  \leanok
  The canonical actual map $F_{n+1}(\Sigma A)\to F_nA$ is a stable equivalence. For sphere generators, its effect is exactly the actual cubical stabilization map.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Construct the map from the padded-tail inclusion and iterated desuspension. The proved quotient shift bijections show it is a stable equivalence; the free/evaluation formula computes its value on the canonical generator.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual stable groups are represented by ordinary source spheres]
  \label{prop:ordinary-source-sphere-representability}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceSpherePrespectrum,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceSphere,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceFreeSphereDegreeIso}

  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableHomotopySourceMap,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceStableHomotopyToHom,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableHomotopySourceMap_bijective}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceStableHomotopyEquiv,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceStableHomotopyEquiv_symm_apply,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceStableHomotopyEquiv_natural}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceStableHomotopyEquiv_freeSphereGenerator,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceSpherePrespectrum_zero,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceSphere_zero}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceSphere_hom_subsingleton_of_stableHomotopy_subsingleton,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableHomotopy_subsingleton_of_sourceSphere_hom_subsingleton}
  \uses{prop:actual-free-sphere-stable-representatives,prop:source-based-homotopy-invariance,prop:free-sphere-stabilization,prop:actual-source-homotopy-functor}
  \leanok
  For every actual prespectrum $X$ and integer $d$, its actual cubical stable homotopy set is naturally bijective to maps from the specified degree-$d$ sphere in the ordinary localization at stable equivalences. The representing sphere is $F_0S^d$ for $d\geq0$ and $F_{-d}S^0$ for $d<0$. The inverse evaluates the map on the specified stable sphere generator.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Normalize free sphere representatives using the actual free stabilization equivalences. Based homotopies and cubical stabilization give equal localized maps, so the construction descends to the actual stable quotient. Extend naturality through localization and evaluate on the actual generator to prove both inverse identities.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual source shifts, negative sphere vanishing, and finite support]
  \label{prop:ordinary-source-sphere-shifts-and-finite-support}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceSpherePrespectrum_positiveIso,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceSphere_actualShiftIso,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceSpherePrespectrum_negSucc}

  \uses{prop:ordinary-source-sphere-representability,prop:ordinary-sphere-connective,prop:actual-free-sphere-stable-representatives}
  \leanok
  The represented source spheres identify with the actual iterated suspension or desuspension of the point-set sphere. Maps from a negative source sphere to the ordinary source sphere form a singleton. If the target levels have based CW homotopy type, every localized sphere map factors through a free spectrum on an actual finite CW model.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Use the explicit free suspension formulas and padded desuspension formulas for the shift comparison. Apply the proved ordinary sphere connectivity through actual representability for negative vanishing, and transport the proved finite support of representatives for the factorization.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual zero objects in the ordinary source category]
  \label{prop:ordinary-source-zero-object}
  \lean{KIP126.Foundation.localization_isZero,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.zeroPrespectrum,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.zeroMap}

  \uses{prop:ordinary-source-sphere-shifts-and-finite-support}
  \leanok
  The actual point prespectrum and its ordinary localization are zero objects, with compatible zero morphisms. Every map from a negative ordinary source sphere to the ordinary source sphere is the actual zero morphism.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  A zero object remains zero under localization: its incoming and outgoing singleton hom-sets descend through the localization universal property. Apply this to the actual point prespectrum, then combine its zero morphisms with the proved negative sphere hom-set singleton.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

  Actual point-set suspension preserves stable equivalences and descends to an autoequivalence of the ordinary source category. Its inverse is the specified padded desuspension. The displayed comparison, unit, and counit are natural on arbitrary localized morphisms.

\begin{proof}
  Make the mapping-path suspension comparison natural, including its bond homotopies. Degree-shift bijections prove preservation of stable equivalences. Descend the actual suspension, desuspension and tail functors through localization, and use the natural padded-tail stable equivalences and suspension comparison to construct the inverse functors.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual stable homotopy degrees under suspension]
  \label{prop:actual-stable-degree-shifts}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableHomotopyDesuspensionEquiv,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableHomotopyTailEquiv,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableHomotopySuspensionEquiv}

  \uses{prop:ordinary-source-suspension-autoequivalence,prop:actual-source-homotopy-functor,prop:source-suspension-desuspension}
  \leanok
  For the actual point-set suspension and padded desuspension, the original stable groups satisfy $\pi_d(\Sigma^n X)\cong\pi_{d-n}(X)$ and $\pi_d(\Sigma^{-n}X)\cong\pi_{d+n}(X)$ as sets. These specified equivalences transport finiteness and vanishing in the stated degrees.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Use the original padded-desuspension representative equivalence and the actual padded-tail stable equivalence. The proved ordinary source suspension--tail isomorphism identifies suspension, and iteration gives the displayed integer degrees.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

  \label{prop:actual-prespectrum-loops}

  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.loopUncurryLast_loopCurryLast,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.loopCurryLast_loopUncurryLast,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.loopUncurryLast_injective}

  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableLoopHomotopyEquiv,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.loopMap_stableEquivalence,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceSuspensionLoopAdjunction}

Its original stable groups satisfy $\pi_d(\Omega X)\cong\pi_{d+1}(X)$ naturally, so it preserves stable equivalences. The actual reduced-suspension functor is left adjoint to this loop functor, both on point-set prespectra and on the original stable localization. In the localization the loop functor is naturally isomorphic to the specified padded desuspension.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Curry in the last cubical coordinate, so the comparison commutes exactly with stabilization in the first coordinate, and descend through the original stable representatives. The quotient universal property and compact-open currying give the strict adjunction. Localize it and use uniqueness of right adjoints to the already proved ordinary suspension equivalence.

\begin{proposition}[Connectivity of the ordinary point-set sphere spectrum]
  \label{prop:ordinary-sphere-connective}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sphereLevel_genLoop_nullhomotopic,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sphereLevel_homotopyGroup_subsingleton,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sphere_stableHomotopy_subsingleton_of_neg}
  \uses{prop:point-set-sphere-geometric-recognition,prop:euclidean-sphere-high-connectivity,prop:prespectrum-cofinal-vanishing}
  \leanok
  The actual ordinary sphere prespectrum satisfies $\pi_d S=0$ for
  every $d<0$. This statement uses its defined cubical stable quotient.
  Passing it to the completed sphere still requires a proof about
  the specified $H\mathbb F_2$ reflection.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  At level $n$, a degree-$d$ representative has dimension $d+n<n$.
  Transport the based Euclidean nullhomotopy through the proved
  homeomorphism, then use cofinal vanishing in the actual stable quotient.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

% SOURCE: PROJECT_BOUNDARY.md, Confirmed design decision 1; MainPaper/main.tex:253-263, 670-758.
\begin{definition}[Stable homotopy context]
  \label{def:stable-context}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.StableHomotopyCategory,KIP126.StableHomotopy.HasFunctorialCofiber,KIP126.StableHomotopy.ClosedSymmetricTensorTriangulated}
  \uses{def:mathlib-shift,def:mathlib-triangle,def:mathlib-pretriangulated,def:mathlib-monoidal-category,def:mathlib-symmetric-category}
  \notready
  A stable homotopy context consists of a pointed additive homotopy category
  of spectra, suspension equivalences $\Sigma^n$, a symmetric monoidal smash
  product with sphere unit $S^0$, cofiber sequences, and distinguished
  triangles.  It includes rotation, functoriality, smash compatibility, and
  the sign and suspension conventions needed below.  Stable homotopy groups
  and their exact sequences are exposed separately so that later proofs do not
  hide them inside this context record.
  Lean provides these abstract structural interfaces. Construction of the
  fixed spectrum category and its specified compatibility witnesses remains
  unfinished; an abstract interface alone does not supply that model.
\end{definition}

% SOURCE: MainPaper/main.tex:126-138 and 253-263; suspension conventions used throughout.
\begin{definition}[Sphere spectrum and suspension]
  \label{def:stable-sphere-suspension}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.SphereSpectrum,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Sphere,KIP126.StableHomotopy.ClosedSymmetricTensorTriangulated.smashSuspIso,KIP126.StableHomotopy.sphereTensorIsoFromRightShift}
  \uses{def:stable-context}
  \notready
  The monoidal unit is the sphere spectrum $S^0$.  For every $n\in\mathbb Z$,
  suspension gives an equivalence $\Sigma^n$ with coherent isomorphisms
  $\Sigma^0X\simeq X$ and $\Sigma^{m+n}X\simeq\Sigma^m\Sigma^nX$, compatible
  with smash products and mapping groups.
  The sphere and these suspension constructions are defined for a supplied
  stable-category interface and its chosen tensor-shift compatibility.
  Their realization in the fixed spectrum model remains unfinished.
\end{definition}

% SOURCE: MainPaper/main.tex:102-170 and throughout Sections 2--7.
\begin{definition}[Stable homotopy groups]
  \label{def:stable-homotopy-group}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.HomotopyGroup,KIP126.StableHomotopy.HomotopyGroups,KIP126.StableHomotopy.homotopyGroupFunctor,KIP126.StableHomotopy.homotopyDesuspend,KIP126.StableHomotopy.homotopyDesuspend_bijective,KIP126.StableHomotopy.homotopyDesuspend_postcompose}
  \uses{def:stable-sphere-suspension}
  \notready
  For a spectrum $X$ and $n\in\mathbb Z$, define
  \[
    \pi_nX=[\Sigma^nS^0,X].
  \]
  These are abelian groups, functorial in $X$, with suspension and composition
  isomorphisms fixed by the context's sign convention.
  Lean already defines these represented groups and proves functoriality,
  bijective desuspension, and its naturality in an abstract stable category.
  The fixed spectrum-category realization is still a separate obligation.
\end{definition}

% SOURCE: MainPaper/main.tex:253-263, global standing assumptions.
\begin{definition}[Admissible spectrum]
  \label{def:admissible-spectrum}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.BoundedBelow,KIP126.StableHomotopy.FiniteMod2Type,KIP126.Classical.Adams.IsENilpotentComplete}
  \uses{def:stable-context}
  \notready
  A spectrum is admissible when it is $2$-complete, connective, and of finite
  type.  Strong convergence is deliberately not part of this predicate:
  whenever a classical or synthetic Adams spectral sequence is invoked, its
  convergence witness is supplied separately.  This avoids defining
  admissibility in terms of spectral sequences that are themselves indexed by
  admissible spectra.
  The linked predicates implement boundedness, degreewise finite mod--$2$
  homology, and completeness of the residual Adams tower. They do not
  constitute a construction of the full admissibility condition on the
  fixed model or its separate convergence witness.
\end{definition}

% SOURCE: MainPaper/main.tex:135-159, where products are used for the sphere;
% standard associative-algebra-object structure in a symmetric monoidal category.
\begin{definition}[Unital multiplication data]
  \label{def:unital-multiplication-data}
  \lean{CategoryTheory.MonObj,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Cohomology.Mod2RingStructure}
  \uses{def:stable-context,def:stable-sphere-suspension}
  \notready
  A unital multiplication datum on a spectrum $R$ is an associative algebra
  object in the stable symmetric monoidal context: it includes multiplication
  $R\wedge R\to R$, a unit $S^0\to R$, and all associativity and unit
  coherences.  This is extra structure and is not implied by admissibility.
  The sphere $S^0$, as the monoidal unit, has its canonical such datum.  The
  same terminology will be used for associative algebra objects in the
  synthetic context.
  Mathlib supplies the abstract algebra-object notion and the unit object's
  canonical multiplication. The project also binds multiplication on a
  supplied mod--$2$ object to its same unit; the required fixed classical
  and synthetic realizations remain unfinished.
\end{definition}

% SOURCE: MainPaper/main.tex:688-769; Pstragowski interface used in Proposition 3.1.
\begin{definition}[$H\mathbb F_2$ homology]
  \label{def:hf2-homology}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Cohomology.Mod2EilenbergMacLane,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Cohomology.Mod2Homology,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Cohomology.mod2HomologyF2Functor,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Cohomology.mod2HomologyConnectingF2}
  \uses{def:stable-context,def:mathlib-f2}
  \notready
  The context contains an Eilenberg--Mac Lane ring spectrum $H\mathbb F_2$ and
  a homology functor $H_*(-;\mathbb F_2)$ from spectra to graded
  $\mathbb F_2$-vector spaces.  It preserves suspension and sends cofiber
  sequences to long exact sequences.
  The represented groups, derived $\mathbb F_2$ scalar structures,
  functorial maps, and connecting maps are implemented for supplied category,
  coefficient-ring, and tensor-exactness data. Construction of those data
  on the fixed spectrum model remains unfinished.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Actual realized simplex attachments and finite skeleta]
  \label{prop:realized-finite-simplicial-skeleta}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.skeleton,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.cellComplex,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.compactSpace_stdSimplex}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.compactSpace_ofSimplex,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.exists_nondegenerate_simplex,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.compactSpace_of_finite}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.finite_skeleton,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.compactSpace_skeleton}
  \leanok
  The specified geometric realization has the actual sequential
  attachment presentation by realized simplex boundaries. Every point
  comes from a nondegenerate simplex. A finite simplicial set has
  compact realization; if every simplicial degree is finite, each
  realized skeleton is compact. At this stage the attaching family
  is still expressed as realized simplex-boundary inclusions.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Apply the realization left adjoint to the proved simplicial
  skeletal attachment diagram. Its colimit over simplex-generated
  subcomplexes gives the point representatives. Finite unions of
  compact simplex images prove compactness; bounded dimension and
  degreewise finiteness make each skeleton finite.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Extension along a shifted cofiber inclusion]
  \label{prop:shifted-cofiber-extension}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.CofiberExtension.shiftedCofiberTriangle,KIP126.StableHomotopy.CofiberExtension.shiftedCofiberTriangle_distinguished,KIP126.StableHomotopy.CofiberExtension.exists_extension_of_shift_comp_zero,KIP126.StableHomotopy.CofiberExtension.exists_eta_nu_extension}
  \uses{def:cofiber-triangle,def:stable-sphere-suspension,def:mathlib-pretriangulated}
  \leanok
  In any supplied stable-homotopy context with the chosen cofibers, let
  $f:X\to Y$, $n\in\mathbb Z$, and $a:\Sigma^nY\to Z$. If
  $a\circ\Sigma^nf=0$, there exists $g:\Sigma^nC(f)\to Z$ with
  $g\circ\Sigma^n\iota_f=a$. All integers and all objects are retained.
  In particular, for the same maps $\eta:S^1\to S^0$ and
  $\nu:S^3\to S^0$, the explicit premise
  $\nu\circ\Sigma^3\eta=0$ gives an extension
  $\Sigma^3C\eta\to S^0$ restricting to $\nu$.
  This does not prove the composite-zero premise for the fixed model,
  choose an actual CW comparison, or identify native coordinates.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Shift the chosen distinguished cofiber triangle, then use the triangle
  isomorphism with components $1,(-1)^n,1$ to make its first two arrows
  positive while retaining the signed connecting map. Contravariant
  representable exactness at the middle term gives the extension.
  No additional sign compatibility is assumed.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Cofiber comparison of an extension and its restriction]
  \label{prop:cofiber-extension-octahedron}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.CofiberExtension.cofiber_triangle_of_extension,KIP126.StableHomotopy.CofiberExtension.exists_eta_nu_cofiber_triangles,KIP126.StableHomotopy.CofiberExtension.sphereSixIso,KIP126.StableHomotopy.CofiberExtension.shiftFourIso}
  \uses{prop:shifted-cofiber-extension,def:mathlib-octahedron}
  \leanok
  In the same category with the octahedron axiom, let
  $g:\Sigma^n C(f)\to Z$ restrict along the positive shifted inclusion
  to $a:\Sigma^nY\to Z$. There are maps
  $m:\Sigma^{n+1}X\to C(a)$ and $j:C(a)\to C(g)$ from one octahedron.
  They give the distinguished triangle
  \[
    C(a)\xrightarrow{j} C(g)
      \xrightarrow{(\Sigma\delta_{f,n})\delta_g}
      \Sigma^{n+2}X\xrightarrow{-\Sigma m}\Sigma C(a),
  \]
  where $\delta_{f,n}$ is the signed connecting map of the normalized
  shifted cofiber triangle. All four octahedron squares are retained,
  including $j\iota_a=\iota_g$ and
  $\delta_gj=(\Sigma^{n+1}\iota_f)\delta_a$.
  No independent choice of a cofiber map replaces $j$.
  For $f=\eta:S^1\to S^0$, $n=3$ and $a=\nu$, the explicit composite-zero
  premise supplies one $g,m,j$ and both triangles
  $S^0\to C(g)\to\Sigma^4C\eta$ and
  $C\nu\to C(g)\to S^6$. The former's second map is exactly
  $\delta_g$ followed by the canonical shift-composition isomorphism.
  Actual composite vanishing and native-module comparisons remain separate.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Apply the octahedron axiom to the rotated normalized shifted triangle,
  the chosen cofiber triangle of $g$, and the chosen cofiber triangle of
  $a$. Rotate its new distinguished triangle and keep the same two maps
  and four squares. The final connecting arrow is $-\Sigma m$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Realized simplex boundaries are actual disk-boundary arrows]
  \label{prop:realized-simplex-boundary-disk}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.subcomplex_realization_injective,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.boundary_realization_range,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.exists_boundary_realization_homeomorph}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.exists_stdSimplex_euclideanBall_homeomorph,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.nonempty_boundary_realization_disk_arrow_iso}
  \uses{prop:geometric-simplex-closed-ball,prop:realized-finite-simplicial-skeleta}
  \leanok
  The realized inclusion $|\partial\Delta[n]|\to|\Delta[n]|$ is isomorphic as an arrow to the Euclidean sphere-to-disk inclusion. Its boundary consists exactly of points with a zero barycentric coordinate.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Represent each subcomplex point in a face and use the positive-coordinate support to prove inclusion injectivity. Compactness gives the boundary homeomorphism; the simplex ball-pair homeomorphism then yields a commuting isomorphism of arrows.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[The actual CW attachment structure on a realization]
  \label{prop:realized-simplicial-cw-structure}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.diskCellComplex,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.cwComplex,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.cwComplexCellsEquiv}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.cwComplexSkeletonIso,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.finite_cwComplex_cells,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.finite_cwComplex_stage_cells}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.finite_cwComplex_skeleton_cells,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.compactSpace_cwComplex_stage}
  \uses{prop:realized-simplex-boundary-disk,prop:realized-finite-simplicial-skeleta}
  \leanok
  Every actual simplicial realization has a categorical CW structure with one cell per nondegenerate simplex. Its filtration is the realized simplicial skeleton. Degreewise finite simplicial sets give finitely many cells at each stage and compact finite stages. Identification with the classical characteristic-map CW structure remains a separate construction.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Reindex the actual simplex attachment pushouts by the commuting disk-boundary arrow isomorphisms. The original sequential colimit is unchanged; the cell and skeleton comparisons are explicit.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Compact generation of actual realizations]
  \label{prop:realization-compactly-generated}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.continuous_iff_simplex,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.realization_uCompactlyGeneratedSpace}
  \uses{prop:realized-finite-simplicial-skeleta}
  \leanok
  A map from the actual realization is continuous exactly when its composites with all realized simplices are continuous. Consequently every realization is compactly generated.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  The realization adjunction preserves the tautological simplex colimit. Standard simplices are compact Hausdorff and therefore already form sufficient compact Hausdorff continuity tests.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Closed embeddings through actual CW attachments]
  \label{prop:relative-cw-closed-embedding}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.isClosed_iff_of_topological_pushout,KIP126.Topology.isClosedEmbedding_of_topological_pushout,KIP126.Topology.isClosedEmbedding_sigma_map}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.isClosedEmbedding_of_coproduct,KIP126.Topology.isClosedEmbedding_of_attachCells,KIP126.Topology.isClosedEmbedding_nat_colimit_of_maps}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.isClosedEmbedding_nat_map_of_succ,KIP126.Topology.isClosedEmbedding_nat_colimit,KIP126.Topology.diskBoundaryInclusion_isClosedEmbedding}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.relativeCWComplex_stage_isClosedEmbedding,KIP126.Topology.relativeCWComplex_inclusion_isClosedEmbedding,KIP126.Topology.relativeCWComplex_isClosedEmbedding}
  \leanok
  An actual relative CW-complex includes its source and every skeleton as closed embedded subspaces of its target. Closed embeddings are preserved by the displayed attachment pushouts, coproducts, and sequential colimits.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Use the actual topological pushout's closed-set criterion and its underlying set pushout to prove closed embedding after attachment. The filtered set-colimit equality criterion proves injectivity of the stages; the final topology proves their images and images of closed subsets are closed.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Homotopy extension through actual CW attachments]
  \label{prop:relative-cw-homotopy-extension}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.HomotopyExtension.pathEvaluationZero,KIP126.Topology.HomotopyExtension.HasHomotopyExtension,KIP126.Topology.HomotopyExtension.hasHomotopyExtension_iff_lifting}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.HomotopyExtension.hasHomotopyExtension_iff_of_arrow_iso,KIP126.Topology.HomotopyExtension.diskBoundaryInclusion_hasHomotopyExtension,KIP126.Topology.HomotopyExtension.relativeCWComplex_hasHomotopyExtension}
  \uses{prop:ball-boundary-homotopy-extension}
  \leanok
  Every actual relative CW-complex inclusion has full homotopy extension for every target space. The extension agrees exactly with the given map at time zero and with the given homotopy on the source.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Currying identifies homotopy extension with left lifting against initial path evaluation. Disk boundaries have this property by the explicit ball retraction. Left lifting is preserved by coproducts, pushouts, and transfinite composition, hence by the given CW attachment presentation.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Realized simplicial inclusions and their actual vertices]
  \label{prop:realized-mono-closed-cofibration}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.relativeCellComplex,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.relativeCWComplex,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.realization_mono_hasHomotopyExtension}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.vertex_homotopy_extension,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.realization_vertex_eq_unit,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.realization_mono_isClosedEmbedding}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.vertex_isClosed}
  \uses{prop:realized-simplex-boundary-disk,prop:relative-cw-homotopy-extension,prop:relative-cw-closed-embedding}
  \leanok
  Every actual simplicial monomorphism realizes to a closed embedding with full homotopy extension. In particular every geometric vertex is closed and has point homotopy extension. Its characteristic-map description agrees exactly with the vertex selected by the realization adjunction.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Realize the actual relative simplex filtration of the monomorphism and replace each attaching arrow by its Euclidean disk-boundary isomorphism. Apply the proved relative CW results. A map from the zero-simplex is split mono; the adjunction unit's naturality identifies its realized point.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Real separation and Hausdorff topology of actual CW realizations]
  \label{prop:actual-cw-hausdorff}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.HomotopyExtension.hasHomotopyExtension_of_coproduct,KIP126.Topology.exists_real_extension_of_homotopyExtension,KIP126.Topology.relativeCWComplex_exists_real_extension}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.exists_real_map_of_pushout,KIP126.Topology.pushout_real_functions_separate_points,KIP126.Topology.normalSpace_sigma}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.exists_real_extension_nat_colimit,KIP126.Topology.real_functions_separate_points_nat_colimit,KIP126.Topology.t2Space_of_real_functions_separate_points}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.relativeCWComplex_stage_real_functions_separate_points,KIP126.Topology.relativeCWComplex_stage_exists_real_extension,KIP126.Topology.relativeCWComplex_real_functions_separate_points}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.cwComplex_t2Space,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.realization_t2Space,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.hf2Realization_t2Space}
  \uses{prop:relative-cw-closed-embedding,prop:relative-cw-homotopy-extension,prop:realized-simplicial-cw-structure}
  \leanok
  Every actual categorical CW-complex is Hausdorff. In particular every simplicial realization and each fixed geometric coefficient level is Hausdorff. Continuous real functions on a relative CW source extend to its target and separate points at each skeletal stage and at the actual colimit.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Use homotopy extension on the homotopy $t f$ to extend a real function. Urysohn separation in the disk coproduct and these extensions separate new and old points in an attachment pushout. Extend separating functions successively and use the actual colimit topology to separate points of the union.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[The actual open part of a cell attachment]
  \label{prop:actual-open-cell-complement}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.isOpen_iff_of_topological_pushout,KIP126.Topology.pushout_cell_eq_of_outside_boundary,KIP126.Topology.isOpenEmbedding_pushout_boundary_complement}
  \uses{prop:relative-cw-closed-embedding}
  \leanok
  In an actual topological attachment pushout, the complement of the attaching-boundary image in the normal Hausdorff cell space maps by an open embedding into the new space.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  The set pushout equality criterion gives injectivity outside the attaching boundary. The actual pushout open-set criterion supplies the induced topology and openness of the image.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Classical CW structure and unique interior representatives]
  \label{prop:actual-classical-cw-realization}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.coproduct_inj_mem_range_iff,KIP126.Topology.isOpenEmbedding_coproduct_inj,KIP126.Topology.coproduct_inj_eq_iff}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.attachCells_interior_eq_iff,KIP126.Topology.attachCells_interior_not_mem_range,KIP126.Topology.isOpenEmbedding_attachCells_boundary_complement}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.simplexDiskHomeomorph,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.simplexCharacteristic,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.simplexCellIndex}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.simplexCharacteristicExtension,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.simplex_interior_isEmbedding,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.exists_nondegenerate_interior}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.nondegenerate_interior_unique,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.realization_isClosed_of_simplex_preimages,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.simplex_boundary_finite_cells}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.simplexCharacteristicExtension_injOn,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.simplexCellChart,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.simplexCellChart_continuousOn_symm}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.simplexCellChart_pairwiseDisjoint,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.simplexCellChart_boundary,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.simplexCellChart_closed}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.simplexCellChart_union,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.realization_nonempty_classicalCWComplex,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.hf2Realization_nonempty_classicalCWComplex}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.hf2RealizationLevel_hasCWType,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.hf2RealizationLevel_isGood}
  \uses{prop:actual-open-cell-complement,prop:actual-cw-hausdorff,prop:hf2-geometric-topology}
  \leanok
  Every actual simplicial realization has the classical CW structure with one cell for each nondegenerate simplex. Every point has a unique nondegenerate interior representative. The fixed geometric coefficient levels have actual based CW type and satisfy the full defined good-space predicate.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Use the actual attachment pushouts to construct embedded cell interiors and exclude the previous skeleton. Boundary descent gives existence and disjoint cells give uniqueness. Each simplex boundary has finitely many lower-dimensional faces. Disk coordinates, inverse continuity, this closure-finiteness and the actual colimit topology verify every classical CW field. Combine this with the proved zero-vertex homotopy extension for the fixed based-space conclusion.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[The concrete Dold--Kan coefficient spaces]
  \label{prop:hf2-dold-kan-construction}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.hf2ChainComplex,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.hf2SimplicialModule,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.hf2SimplicialSet}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.hf2RealizationLevel,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.hf2NormalizedComplexIso,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.hf2NormalizedHomologySelfIso}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.hf2ChainComplex_finite,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.hf2SimplicialModule_finite,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.hf2SimplicialSet_finite}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.hf2SimplicialModule_isZero_below,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.hf2SimplicialSet_subsingleton_below,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.hf2NormalizedHomology_isZero}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.hf2NormalizedHomology_subsingleton}
  \uses{def:point-set-prespectrum,def:mathlib-f2}
  \leanok
  For every $n\geq0$, form the actual chain complex
  $\mathbb F_2[n]$, its Dold--Kan denormalization, its underlying
  simplicial set, and the geometric realization based at its zero
  vertex. Every set of simplices is finite, and below degree $n$ it
  contains only the zero simplex. Normalized homology is
  $\mathbb F_2$ in degree $n$ and zero elsewhere. These are algebraic
  homology calculations; spectrum structure maps and comparison with
  cubical homotopy are further constructions.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  The Dold--Kan counit identifies the normalized complex with the
  specified single-degree complex. Its explicit finite coproduct
  formula in each simplicial degree proves finiteness and the
  low-dimensional vanishing.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Topology and based cell structure of the coefficient levels]
  \label{prop:hf2-geometric-topology}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.hf2RealizationLevel_uCompactlyGeneratedSpace,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.hf2RealizationLevel_compactSpace_skeleton,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.hf2RealizationLevel_isWellBased}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.hf2Realization_nonempty_cwComplex,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.hf2Realization_finite_cw_skeleton,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.hf2Realization_compact_skeleton}
  \uses{prop:hf2-dold-kan-construction,prop:realization-compactly-generated,prop:realized-finite-simplicial-skeleta,prop:realized-simplicial-cw-structure,prop:realized-mono-closed-cofibration}
  \leanok
  Each actual coefficient level $|\Gamma(\mathbb F_2[n])|$ is compactly generated and well-based at its specified zero vertex. Each finite skeleton is compact and has a categorical CW structure with finitely many cells.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Apply the realization topology and relative CW theorems to the proved degreewise finite Dold--Kan simplicial sets and their specified zero vertices.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual continuous coefficient spectrum structure maps]
  \label{prop:hf2-actual-structure-maps}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.HF2Bond.decalage,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.HF2Bond.decalageProjection,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.HF2Bond.conePoint}

  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.HF2Bond.loopBond_one,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.HF2Bond.loopBond_point,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.HF2Bond.coefficientSimplex}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.HF2Bond.coefficientLoop,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.HF2Bond.coefficientNormalizedLoop,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.HF2Bond.coefficientLoopChainMap}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.HF2Bond.coefficientToLoop,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.HF2Bond.coefficientToLoopSet,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.HF2Bond.bond}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.HF2Bond.bond_zero,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.HF2Bond.bond_one,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.HF2Bond.bond_point}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.hf2RealizationPrespectrum,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.HF2Bond.coefficientToLoop_injective}

\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}

The Dold--Kan correspondence sends the displayed normalized coefficient map to the required simplicial map; normalization and monomorphism preservation prove its degreewise injectivity.

\end{proof}

\end{proof}

  Glue compatible maps on a finite closed cover. Apply the cell extensions simultaneously in each total-degree grid layer; different new cells intersect inside the old domain, so the lifts agree.   Use the finite-factor product comparison and equalizer description for the pullback. Factor the compact cubical homotopy through a genuine finite target subcomplex, transport its prescribed horn lift into the actual pullback, lift there, and project back. A finite simplicial set has a dimension bound, at which its skeleton equals the whole simplicial set.

\end{proof}

  The fixed Dold--Kan geometric prespectrum is an actual source $H\mathbb F_2$ model: its levels are good based spaces, its genuine stable homotopy is zero in every nonzero integer degree, and its degree-zero stable homotopy is $\mathbb F_2$. The coefficient equivalence preserves the normalized generator, zero, and the actual first-coordinate loop concatenation. The public fixed object \texttt{sourceHF2} is bound directly to this displayed Dold--Kan model; the existence declaration is witnessed by that same object. Both use only standard Lean axioms.

  The zeroth degeneracy splits the actual path projection, so the proved realization lifting theorem applies. Its contractible total space and actual strict fiber identify every specified bonding map as an isomorphism on cubical homotopy. Iterate to the discrete zero space or the connected positive-level zeroth homotopy set. Descend these compatible computations through the actual stable quotient; the two-element group calculation verifies concatenation and zero. Populate every unchanged field of the original source-model structure.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

  The constant representative is the zero of the constructed stable group, so the original normalization preserves zero. A zero-preserving bijection between an additive group and the two-element field preserves addition. Apply this elementary group calculation to the unchanged coefficient equivalence.

\end{proof}

The shift used in this calculation is definitionally the original fixed source shift.

  Apply the actual degree-shift equivalences to the proved source coefficient characterization. The zero-degree coefficient equivalence and nonzero-degree vanishing give the finite cardinalities.

\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Use the proved additive equivalence between the actual stable group of a finite product and the product of its stable groups, followed by the actual coefficient-shift computation.

\end{proof}

  Realization of the simplicial cylinder $K\times\Delta[1]$ is naturally homeomorphic to $|K|\times I$. A continuous map from this cylinder into a space has an actual map into the singular realization, with exact evaluation, endpoint formulas, constant-map behavior, and naturality in both variables. Singular realization and evaluation are functorial for actual based maps.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Use the proved product homeomorphism for a standard simplex and the explicit interval identification. Apply the realization--singular adjunction to the cylinder map; its triangle identities and naturality give the stated formulas.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Exact cubical lifting through singular evaluation]
  \label{prop:actual-singular-evaluation-surjectivity}

  \leanok
  For every based space and every nonnegative dimension, each actual based cubical loop lifts exactly through evaluation from its singular realization. Consequently evaluation is surjective on every actual homotopy set, including degree zero.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Identify the simplex--boundary pair with the cube--boundary pair. The given loop becomes one singular simplex, whose realized characteristic map provides the lift. Constancy on the boundary follows by adjunction naturality; the triangle identity gives exact evaluation.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

  \label{prop:actual-singular-relative-comparison}

  For maps that are realizations of actual simplicial maps into a singular set, evaluation reflects continuous homotopies relative to the image of any realized simplicial map. For arbitrary points of a singular realization, evaluation reflects path-connectedness; consequently it induces a bijection on the actual degree-zero homotopy set.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Lift the relative homotopy through the actual singular cylinder. Adjunction uniqueness identifies the prescribed realized endpoints and makes the lifted homotopy constant on the specified image. Every realized point joins a vertex in its characteristic simplex, and a path in the original space lifts between the corresponding singular vertices.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

  Every actual prespectrum has a functorial prespectrum whose levels are its good based singular realizations. The original structure maps determine strict singular structure maps and a natural actual evaluation map to the original prespectrum. This evaluation is surjective on every original integer stable homotopy group.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Apply the actual singular cylinder to each original structure map after level evaluation. Its endpoint, basepoint and naturality formulas verify all strict prespectrum equations. Exact loop lifting gives level homotopy surjectivity, and the proved cofinal representative argument gives stable surjectivity.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

  A compact map into a based realization factors through a finite simplicial subcomplex containing its specified vertex. Every actual cubical loop factors as a based loop through such a subcomplex. A based homotopy and both endpoints factor through one finite subcomplex, with all boundary equalities preserved exactly.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Adjoin the vertex simplex to the proved finite support. The realized inclusion is an embedding and preserves the displayed vertex, so it reflects the exact boundary equalities for both loops and their homotopies.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

  Every positive-dimensional realized simplicial horn is a retract of its actual simplex. For any topological space $B$, the original singular simplicial set $\operatorname{Sing}B$ satisfies the Kan condition. Every continuous map from a realized simplicial set extends across the realization of any anodyne extension; consequently every such realization has a continuous retraction.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Subtract the minimum coordinate outside the omitted vertex and add the removed mass to that vertex. This fixes the geometric horn and gives its explicit continuous retraction. The realization--singular adjunction gives the actual singular fillers and then transfers their lifting property to the continuous extension statement.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

If $|T|\le N$, every two convex combinations along the same nested flag have distance at most $(1-1/N)D$. The factor is in $[0,1)$, and the formulas agree with the actual standard-simplex coordinate barycenters.
\end{proposition}

  Split the larger vertex mean into the smaller face and its complement. Average the pairwise distance bound, then average again for convex combinations of flag barycenters. The standard-coordinate formula follows by evaluating each vertex sum.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\end{proof}

  The positive-coordinate stars form an actual open cover of a finite order-complex realization. A nonempty finite star intersection has comparable vertex support; the intersection for a nonempty chain contracts explicitly to its uniform barycenter, fixing that barycenter. Every open cover is refined by the images of all stars at some finite actual subdivision depth. Consequently every continuous map between finite order-complex realizations has, after a finite subdivision, a vertex assignment carrying entire source stars into target stars; this assignment is forced at points mapping to a target vertex.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Use the actual coordinate homeomorphism and affine contraction on chain-supported points. The small-simplex result bounds star diameters through a common vertex. Compactness and a Lebesgue number give a finite subdivision refining the cover. Apply this to inverse images of target stars and use the exact coordinate support of a vertex.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

  A vertex assignment carrying each source star into its assigned target star carries chains to chains. It defines an actual simplicial map from the source chain subdivision to the target order nerve. Its realization is homotopic to the original continuous map composed with the actual subdivision homeomorphism, preserving every zero coordinate. The homotopy fixes any specified subset on which that original composite takes vertex values.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Test the star condition at actual positive barycenters to see that each finite chain image is comparable. Take the maximum of that image to obtain a monotone map on nonempty chains. The explicit coordinate formula gives support containment, so the affine coordinate homotopy stays in the order complex and is constant wherever its target is a vertex.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

The homotopy preserves all zero target coordinates and fixes every point sent to a target vertex. It is therefore relative to any specified vertex-valued subset. Repeated singleton chains specify actual base vertices at every subdivision depth, and the approximation preserves a specified basepoint exactly.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Obtain the actual star assignment from finite refinement and apply the actual simplicial carrier construction. The carrier homotopy preserves coordinate support. Compute the image of each singleton-chain vertex under every subdivision homeomorphism to identify the specified basepoint.

  Suborders give actual simplicial monomorphisms, whose realization images are exactly the points with zero coordinates outside the suborder. The actual subdivision homeomorphism respects these inclusions. The suborder of proper nonempty chains realizes to the entire coordinate boundary. For a finite linear order, this gives actual homeomorphisms to the standard simplex and its entire boundary, retaining the geometric barycentric projection formula.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Compute coordinates under the vertex inclusion and use injectivity of the actual coordinate homeomorphism. A positive coordinate at the full chain prevents any original coordinate from vanishing; removing that vertex gives precisely the boundary. In a linear order every support is a chain, so the full coordinate image is the standard simplex.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Singular evaluation reflects finite-order-factorized nullhomotopies]
