  Singular evaluation reflects homotopies between actual realized simplicial maps. If a continuous map factors through a finite order-complex realization followed by the realization of an actual simplicial map to the singular set, evaluation reflects its nullhomotopy. The same holds relative to a specified base vertex when the continuous factor preserves that vertex. The intermediate continuous factor need not be simplicial.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Apply the actual finite-order simplicial approximation to the continuous factor. The exact singular-cylinder endpoint laws reflect the resulting evaluated homotopy between realized simplicial maps. Transfer back through the actual subdivision homeomorphism, using its precise basepoint formula in the relative case.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

Their pointwise order comparison defines an actual simplicial prism map; the proved realization-cylinder homeomorphism gives its continuous relative homotopy.

  Compatible homotopies on the pieces of an actual topological pushout glue to a homotopy on the pushout, retaining prescribed relative regions. Along a simplicial monomorphism, a continuous homotopy between evaluated singular boundary maps can be replaced by an actual simplicial map with the prescribed boundary exactly, together with the specified evaluated boundary homotopy. Hence maps into the singular set glue across an actual simplicial pushout when their evaluated boundary maps agree up to homotopy: one piece is retained exactly, and the other changes by a homotopy, also relative to any prescribed simplicial region where the boundary homotopy is fixed.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Curry the piecewise homotopies into the compact-open path space and use the actual pushout universal property before uncurrying. The proved realization cofibration extends the given boundary homotopy; the realization-singular adjunction makes its final map simplicial with the exact prescribed boundary. Apply the singular-cylinder comparison to retain the relative homotopy and use the actual simplicial pushout to glue the resulting strict maps.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

  Every chain lies in the cylinder or the source cone: inspect its last vertex and use the defining apex order relation. A chain in both pieces lies exactly in the source. The parameterization is surjective and a quotient map; its only identifications collapse the entire top end. Every homeomorphism of the bases extends to a homeomorphism of the cones.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Write the coordinates as $(1-t)$ times the base coordinates with apex coordinate $t$. Compute the fibers exactly, prove surjectivity by normalization away from the apex, and use compactness and Hausdorffness for the quotient property. Descend the product of the base map and the height coordinate. Descending its inverse proves the homeomorphism statement.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Compact maps and relative homotopies factor through a finite stage]
  \label{prop:actual-sequential-compact-factorization}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.range_nat_colimit_mono,KIP126.Topology.finite_subset_range_nat_colimit,KIP126.Topology.isCompact_subset_range_nat_colimit}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.continuousMap_factor_nat_colimit,KIP126.Topology.continuousMap_factor_nat_colimit_after,KIP126.Topology.homotopyRel_factor_nat_colimit}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.homotopicRel_nat_colimit_iff}
  \uses{prop:relative-cw-closed-embedding}
  \leanok
  For a sequence of $T_1$ spaces with closed embedding transition maps and its actual topological colimit, every compact subset lies in one finite stage. Every continuous map from a compact space factors continuously through a stage beyond any prescribed index. The same holds for relative homotopies. Consequently two stage maps from a compact space become relatively homotopic in the colimit exactly when they do so in a later finite stage.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  If a compact subset escaped every stage, choose successively escaping points. Every subset of these points intersects each finite stage in a finite closed set, so the colimit topology would make the selected infinite set closed and discrete, contradicting compactness. The proved stage embeddings turn set-theoretic factorization into continuous factorization. Apply this to the compact image of the homotopy cylinder and use injectivity to retain endpoints and the entire relative region.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

The homeomorphism restricts to the specified iterated subdivision comparison on the boundary and sends the apex to the barycenter.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  The proper-chain boundary and its iterated subdivisions remain finite and nonempty. Extend the actual iterated boundary homeomorphism over cones, then compose with the proved boundary-cone simplex homeomorphism. The two exact base and apex formulas compose to the required identities.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\end{proof}

  Compute the actual point-set comparison using simplices and colimits. Factor the second projection of a compact map through a finite simplicial subobject, where the proved realization product homeomorphism applies. Apply this to the entire compact homotopy cylinder; injectivity retains its endpoints and the prescribed relative subset.

\end{proof}

  For a finite order $P$, the bottom and side of the actual order-cone cylinder form the realization of a specified finite order horn. Its nerve and realization are the actual pushouts of those two pieces along their corner. The realized horn inclusion has an actual continuous retraction that fixes the whole horn. \begin{proof}

Realization preserves this pushout and identifies the order cylinder with the actual product. Apply the proved homotopy-extension property to this particular horn to construct the retraction; pushout extensionality proves its restriction is the identity. Glue the given bottom map and canonical side prism on the horn, then compose with this actual retraction.

\end{proof}

No finite-order factorization of the original continuous map is assumed. The top face is allowed to change.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Use the actual simplex or disk-cone homeomorphisms and the affine contraction to compare the identity to the constructed horn retraction while fixing its image. Both the original map and the realized evaluated adjoint agree on that actual horn by the adjunction identities. Compose their two deformation homotopies relative to the horn.

\end{proof}

The specified source point supplies the dimension-zero argument.

Actual cubical replacements have this property, and literal mapping cones of maps between such objects retain it. Their stable source maps into the stabilized T1 replacement have strict representatives, equal exactly when the strict maps are homotopic.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Apply the actual lifting property to the replacement comparison to obtain its strict section. Lift the cone base map, then lift its nullhomotopy with both endpoints fixed. The strict section and actual homotopy lifting transfer the proved mapping-class representation to every object satisfying this geometric lifting property.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Replace the target by its actual stabilized loop replacement and use the strict cone universal property on a represented nullhomotopy. Iterated cone comparisons identify the other terms in the five-term exact sequence with actual suspensions. The abelian-group five lemma and representable detection prove stable invariance.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

Apply the proved exactness to successive cones and the actual Puppe comparisons for three-position detection. The actual quotient-cofiber pushout comparison then proves stable excision, retaining all cofibrancy and homotopy-extension premises.

Compatible maps out of the sequence extend over that cofiber. The stable homotopy groups of the geometric telescope give the colimit of those same original stage groups.
\end{proposition}

  Form the actual coproduct difference and its chosen source cofiber. Exactness of the represented cofiber sequence extends any compatible family. Apply it to the actual geometric telescope cocone, and use its previously proved compact stable representative theorem for the group colimit.

Compose three actual pushouts along an old-space equivalence and its inverse; the two composite comparisons are homotopic to identity comparisons. Explicit two-out-of-six constructs the desired inverse. The actual order prism gives the fiber deformation of the order mapping cylinder and supplies the order-cone comparison.

For every strict prespectrum sequence, the previously specified stage-preserving comparison from its actual Milnor cofiber to its actual geometric telescope is an isomorphism in the original stable localization.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Stable homotopy preserves the actual countable coproducts. The injective direct-sum difference in the adjacent degree kills the boundary in the actual long exact sequence, so the original cofiber map becomes the genuine cokernel. Its proved cokernel universal property gives the original sequence colimit. Both telescope group cocones are therefore colimits, and their original comparison preserves every stage map. Colimit uniqueness and the proved conservativity of original stable homotopy give the source isomorphism.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual finite-stage detection and telescope convergence]
  \label{prop:actual-milnor-finite-stage-detection}

  Every stable class of the actual Milnor telescope comes from a finite stage. A stage class maps to zero exactly when it becomes zero at a later stage. Eventual degreewise vanishing gives vanishing in the telescope, and common lower bounds are retained. A compatible map from the telescope which is eventually bijective on each original stable degree is an isomorphism. If every transition is bijective in a degree, each original stage inclusion is bijective in that degree.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Apply the concrete filtered-set colimit representation and equality criterion to the proved original stable-group colimit. Pass to a common later stage to detect equality and zero. Degreewise eventual bijectivity gives bijections on the actual colimit; the original stable homotopy functor detects the resulting source isomorphism.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

  The specified degree-$n$ source sphere has zero stable homotopy below $n$, for every integer $n$. The actual coproduct of these spheres indexed by the original degree-$n$ classes maps surjectively onto that stable group. Taking the actual coproduct over all classes in all degrees at least $n$ gives a cover surjective in every such degree and zero below $n$. The original inclusion into its chosen source cofiber kills every class at or above $n$ and is bijective on every original stable group below $n$.
\end{proposition}

  Transport actual sphere connectivity through its proved suspension and padded-desuspension comparisons. Original sphere representability defines each summand map and identifies the image of its specified generator. The proved small coproduct preservation gives the lower vanishing bound. The actual cofiber long exact sequence proves high-degree killing and low-degree bijectivity.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual upper cell orthogonality]
  \label{prop:actual-upper-cell-orthogonality}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceSphere_addShiftIso,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceUpperSphereCover_hom_subsingleton,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceUpperSphereCover_shift_hom_subsingleton}

  \uses{prop:actual-upper-sphere-cell-killing}
  \leanok
  The specified representing spheres commute with all integer shifts. Upper sphere covers and their nonnegative shifts have no maps into an object whose original stable groups vanish at or above the cutoff. The actual cell-killing unit, and its suspension, induce bijections on maps into every such target.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Use the original sphere representability and the shifted coproduct universal property. The actual cofiber exact sequence gives existence and uniqueness of extension; the signed shifted triangle gives the adjacent shifted statement.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual represented Milnor extension and uniqueness]
  \label{prop:actual-milnor-represented-extension}

  \uses{prop:actual-source-milnor-colimit-comparison}
  \leanok
  For an actual source sequence, surjective precomposition along its transitions makes the represented one-minus-shift surjective. Surjectivity for the shifted transitions makes actual telescope maps determined by all stage restrictions. If unshifted transition precomposition is bijective and shifted transition precomposition is surjective, restriction to the original zeroth stage is bijective.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Solve the represented difference recursion, then use actual cofiber exactness for uniqueness and the compatible family extension for existence.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

  For every original source spectrum and every integer cutoff, iterate the actual upper cell-killing construction and take its actual Milnor telescope. Its specified unit is bijective on the original stable groups below the cutoff; groups at or above it vanish. Maps through this unit into every upper-truncated object are uniquely determined. These constructions define an additive truncation functor, natural unit, coherent cutoff restrictions and the actual Postnikov inverse tower; every restriction is surjective on every original stable group.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Finite-stage detection kills each upper class at its next stage, while the lower groups remain unchanged. The proved represented Milnor extension theorem gives the unit universal property. Define maps and cutoff restrictions by that unique extension and prove all functorial and naturality laws by uniqueness. Below the lower cutoff both units identify the groups; above it the target group is zero.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual stable degree orthogonality]
  \label{prop:actual-postnikov-degree-orthogonality}

  \leanok
  Truncating an object below its lower stable connectivity bound gives a zero object. Thus there are no nonzero maps from an object vanishing below an integer cutoff into one vanishing at or above it.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Every original stable group of the truncation is zero, so actual stable-group conservativity detects the zero object. Apply the constructed truncation unit universal property.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

  Use the actual compatible units to construct the map into the homotopy limit. In each fixed degree the tower is eventually the unchanged original group with bijective transitions. The actual homotopy-limit group theorem identifies that degree, and original stable-group conservativity proves the specified map is an isomorphism.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

For every original source map, inverse rotation of its chosen actual cofiber defines a specified fiber triangle and inclusion. Surjectivity of the map in degree $d+1$ makes this actual inclusion injective in degree $d$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Transport the representing spheres through the fully faithful coherent shift and verify its naturality for the actual original map. Surjectivity makes the adjacent shifted cofiber map vanish; exactness of the twice inverse-rotated distinguished triangle gives injectivity, retaining the original signs and coherence maps.
  \leanok

\begin{proposition}[Actual Postnikov layers and original stable groups]
  \label{prop:actual-postnikov-layers}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourcePostnikovLayer,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourcePostnikovLayerInclusion,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourcePostnikovLayerHomotopyEquiv}

  \leanok
  The degree-$n$ layer is the actual chosen fiber of the specified restriction from cutoff $n+1$ to cutoff $n$. Its original stable groups vanish outside degree $n$. Its specified inclusion is injective in every degree and bijective in degree $n$; composing this bijection with the inverse original truncation unit gives an additive equivalence with the original degree-$n$ stable group.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  All restriction maps are surjective on all original groups, so the actual fiber inclusion is injective. Below $n$ the restriction is bijective, and above $n$ the upper truncation vanishes. Actual fiber exactness gives the bijection in degree $n$ and the original additive comparisons give the displayed group equivalence.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual maps detected by a single stable degree]
  \label{prop:actual-postnikov-map-detection}

  \uses{prop:actual-postnikov-degree-orthogonality,prop:actual-upper-sphere-cell-killing}
  \leanok
  For an object vanishing below $n$, the cofiber of its actual degree-$n$ sphere cover vanishes below $n+1$. A map from such an object to one vanishing above $n$ is determined by its original degree-$n$ stable-group map. Consequently, on an object concentrated in degree $n$, a scalar annihilating that group annihilates the actual identity morphism.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  The sphere cover surjects onto the original group, so its actual cofiber has the improved lower bound. Degree orthogonality kills maps from that cofiber into the target. Precomposition with the cover therefore detects the original map, and sphere representability detects each summand. Apply this faithfulness to the scalar identity and zero map.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

The proved actual Postnikov convergence identifies the original object with the homotopy limit of those local truncations; actual Milnor locality and isomorphism closure finish the argument.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual finite fibers over full-dimensional simplex points]
  \label{prop:actual-full-support-finite-fibers}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.Simplex.positiveSupport,KIP126.Topology.Simplex.mem_positiveSupport,KIP126.Topology.Simplex.positiveSupport_map}

  \leanok
  Positive support of an actual simplex point behaves exactly under a vertex map. If source support has cardinal at most $n+1$ and the target point has support of cardinal $n+1$, each fixed source support contains at most one preimage. Consequently an actual order-realization map from a finite order complex of dimension at most $n$ has finite fiber over such a full-dimensional target point.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Full target support forces the vertex map on any possible source support to be bijective. The actual affine coordinate formula therefore determines every source coordinate. There are only finitely many possible source supports, and the actual order-complex coordinate homeomorphism transfers the finite-fiber conclusion.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual neighborhood deformation from closed homotopy extension]
  \label{prop:actual-closed-hep-neighborhood-deformation}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.HomotopyExtension.HasHomotopyExtension.exists_ndr}

  \leanok
  Every closed homotopy-extension inclusion $A\to B$ has an actual continuous function $u:B\to[0,1]$ with zero set exactly its image and an actual homotopy starting at the identity, fixing that image, and sending every point with $u<1$ into it at time one. No metrizability or normality is imposed on $B$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Take the actual cylinder retraction $r$ and let $u(b)$ be the supremum norm of the continuous function $t\mapsto t-r_2(b,t)$. Compactness of the interval gives continuity of this norm. Closedness of the image identifies its zero set by taking the limit from positive times. The first coordinate of the original retraction supplies the stated deformation.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

No normality or metrizability is imposed on the ambient spaces.

  Construct product neighborhood functions and the relative deformation times explicitly. At their common zero locus, the deformation is constant in its compact time parameter; a tube argument proves continuity even when that parameter varies discontinuously. The resulting neighborhood deformation gives the actual cylinder retraction. Apply this construction to the original inclusion and the two interval endpoints.

\end{proof}

A square induces a stable equivalence of cones whenever its two object maps are stable equivalences; any two of these three equivalences imply the third. A strict map is a stable equivalence exactly when its literal cone has zero original stable groups.   The original cubical sphere has a specified pointed one-point compactification chart. Every invertible affine map in these coordinates induces an original sphere self-map whose stable class is the original generator or its negative.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Retain the actual cube chart, transport the explicit affine isotopy, and compute each coordinate reflection using the original loop inverse. Compositions of coordinate signs give the stated signed generator.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Affine independence of actual iterated subdivision vertices]
  \label{prop:actual-subdivision-vertex-affine-independence}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.Simplex.affineIndependent_of_injective_simplex_sum,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.iteratedOrderSubdivisionVertices_affineIndependent,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.iteratedOrderSubdivision_coordinateVertices_affineIndependent}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.orderBoundary_sum_smul_barycenter,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.iteratedOrderBoundaryVertices_affineIndependent}
  \uses{prop:actual-full-star-coverings}
  \leanok
  The original recursive barycentric vertices of each nondegenerate simplex in any iterated subdivision of the standard boundary are affinely independent. Their sum-one combinations retain the original coordinate realization formula.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Injectivity of the actual simplex combination map implies affine independence by shifting an affine dependence into positive simplex coordinates. Apply the proved coordinate homeomorphisms and their recursive barycenter formulas.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual full-star sheets with their original coordinates]
  \label{prop:actual-full-star-sheets}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.orderFullStarSheets,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.orderFullStarSheets_finite,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.orderFullStarSheets_topologicalSpace}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.orderFullStarSheets_discreteTopology,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.orderFullStarSheet_injOn,KIP126.Topology.SimplicialRealization.existsUnique_orderFullStarSheet_of_preimage}

  \uses{prop:actual-full-star-coverings}
  \leanok
  The inverse image of a full target star under the original finite-dimensional order map is homeomorphic to that star times its discrete set of actual source-support sheets. For finite source this sheet set is finite. The first coordinate is the original map; each sheet retains the exact source positive support and the original coordinate inverse formula.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Use full-support cardinality and vertex injectivity to identify the unique source-support sheet. Assemble the original full-star homeomorphisms over the disjoint open source stars, retaining their coordinate formulas.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual affine equivalences matching independent vertices]
  \label{prop:actual-independent-span-affine-equivalence}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.Simplex.independentSpanBasis,KIP126.Topology.Simplex.independentSpanAffineEquiv,KIP126.Topology.Simplex.independentSpanAffineEquiv_vertex}
  \leanok
  Two nonempty equally indexed independent families of real module points determine an actual affine equivalence between their affine spans, carrying each specified source vertex to its specified target vertex.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Regard each family as a basis of its own affine span. Choose one common index, identify the corresponding difference bases, and translate back. The basis formulas prove the exact vertex equations.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual scalar telescope detects scalar completeness]
  \label{prop:actual-scalar-telescope-completeness-detection}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceScalarSequence_step,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceScalarSequence_oneSubShift,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceScalarTelescope_scalar_isIso}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceScalarTelescopeUnit_precomp_bijective,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceScalarTelescopeUnit_shift_precomp_bijective,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceScalarSequence}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceScalarTelescope,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceScalarTelescopeUnit,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceMooreCompletionUnit_isIso_of_scalarSphereTelescope_orthogonal}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceIsScalarComplete_iff_scalarSphereTelescope_orthogonal}
  \uses{prop:scalar-residual-limit,prop:actual-milnor-represented-extension,prop:actual-source-moore-universal}
  \leanok
  The actual Milnor telescope of repeated multiplication by a natural scalar has invertible scalar action. Its original unit induces bijections into scalar-invertible targets, including all integer shifts. Orthogonality to all shifts of the actual scalar sphere telescope is equivalent to orthogonality to all scalar-invertible objects, and makes the actual Moore completion unit an isomorphism.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Apply the scalar-cofiber inverse calculation to the actual coproduct difference. The represented Milnor universal property gives the unit bijections. For the Moore-unit cofiber these and the actual triangle give vanishing of all original stable groups, hence a zero cofiber and an invertible unit.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

  The specified comparison from the actual dyadic scalar telescope of the original sphere to the original doubled-bond sphere is an isomorphism in the source stable localization, retaining its original stage maps.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Construct the original cubical stage maps and prove their doubling equations. Lift the original doubled-bond representatives through the scalar telescope, respecting both original stable relations. Finite-stage representation and invertibility of doubling give inverse bijections in every degree, and stable homotopy detects the isomorphism.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual finite simplicial realization preserves stable smash]
  \label{prop:actual-finite-simplicial-realization-flatness}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.BasedSpace.adjoinPointFunctor,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.BasedSpace.adjoinPointAdjunction,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.BasedSpace.adjoinPointFunctor_preservesColimits}

  \uses{prop:actual-finite-parameter-cell-flatness,prop:realized-simplex-boundary-disk,prop:realized-finite-simplicial-skeleta,prop:realization-compactly-generated}
  \leanok
  Adjoining a disjoint basepoint is an actual left adjoint. The realized standard simplex boundary inclusion is isomorphic as an arrow to the full cube boundary inclusion, retaining both maps. Consequently smashing with the realization of any actual finite simplicial set with a disjoint basepoint preserves every original stable equivalence, without any condition on the input spectrum levels.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Construct the adjoin-point adjunction and the actual boundary arrow comparison. Transport finite simplicial cell attachments through the two left adjoints to actual cubical attachments, and apply finite cell induction with the proved arbitrary-input gluing theorem.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual wedge finite support up to relative homotopy]
  \label{prop:actual-wedge-homotopy-finite-support}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.basepointNeighborhoodKernel,KIP126.Topology.isCompact_wedge_finite_distinguishable_support,KIP126.Topology.continuousMap_wedge_finite_distinguishable_support}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.wedgeSubfamilyInclusion_retraction_mem_open,KIP126.Topology.wedgeSubfamilyRetraction_comp_preimage_subset,KIP126.Topology.continuousMap_wedge_homotopy_of_distinguishable_support}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.continuousMap_wedge_homotopy_finite_support}
  \uses{prop:actual-wedge-compact-finite-support,prop:actual-specialization-relative-homotopy}
  \leanok
  A continuous map from a compact space into an arbitrary actual quotient wedge is homotopic to its finite-subfamily retraction followed by inclusion. The homotopy fixes every point at which the original map already agrees with this retraction. No T1 or compact-generation hypothesis on the summands is required.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Compactness leaves only finitely many summands where the image exits the intersection of all basepoint neighborhoods. On the remaining summands the finite retraction specializes the original value. The resulting endpoint jump is continuous and fixed on the equality locus.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual specialization relative homotopy]
  \label{prop:actual-specialization-relative-homotopy}
  \lean{KIP126.Topology.homotopyRelOfOpenPreimageSubset}
  \leanok
  If the inverse image of every open set under one continuous map is contained in its inverse image under a second, the second map is homotopic to the first relative to their equality locus.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Keep the second map before the final time and use the first at the endpoint. The open-preimage containment proves joint continuity and the equality locus is fixed.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual arbitrary coproduct and cell-family stable smash]
  \label{prop:actual-arbitrary-coproduct-and-cell-flatness}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableHomotopy_coproduct_finite_support_general,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.coproductMap_stableEquivalence_general,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableEquivalence_of_cofan_map_general}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.spaceSmashMap_cubeAttachCells_stableEquivalence_general}
  \uses{prop:actual-wedge-homotopy-finite-support,prop:actual-finite-parameter-cell-flatness,prop:actual-general-parameter-cell-stability}
  \leanok
  Every stable class of an arbitrary original strict coproduct has finite support after an actual based homotopy. Arbitrary original coproducts therefore preserve stable equivalences without T1, compact-generation or cofibrancy assumptions on the input spectra. An arbitrary family of cubical parameter attachments extends stable smash invariance from the old parameter to the new one under the stated compact-generation hypothesis on that parameter.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Apply compact relative homotopy support to the original stable loop, keeping its full based boundary. Finite-subfamily retractions and finite coproduct stability give the arbitrary coproduct result. Use the actual boundary and cube coproducts, their based HEP, and actual pushout gluing for the attachment family.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Reflection of an already local spectrum]
  \label{prop:local-spectrum-reflection-unit}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.CompletionModel.unit_isIso_of_local,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.CompletionModel.unit_hf2Equivalence,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.IsHF2Local.isIso_of_hf2Equivalence}
  \uses{prop:actual-hf2-source-recognition}
  \leanok
  For a supplied $H\mathbb F_2$-local reflection and an already local
  point-set spectrum $X$, its reflection unit is an isomorphism in the
  ordinary stable category.
  Testing the universal property against every shift of $H\mathbb F_2$
  also proves that the unit is a mod-two cohomology equivalence.
  Such an equivalence between two local objects is invertible.
  The proof uses the supplied reflection universal property twice.
  Existence of the fixed reflection remains a separate construction.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Stable homotopy of reflection units on local objects]
  \label{prop:source-reflection-homotopy}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.CompletionModel.unit_stableEquivalence_of_local,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceHF2Shift_isLocal,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceHF2_isLocal}

  \uses{prop:actual-source-homotopy-functor,prop:local-spectrum-reflection-unit}
  \leanok
  The actual point-set reflection unit of an already local object
  is a stable equivalence. In particular the source coefficient spectrum
  and its specified integer source shifts are local, and reflection
  preserves their actual stable homotopy. The nonzero-degree stable
  groups of the reflected coefficient spectrum vanish.
  These statements are proved for every supplied reflection, using the
  constructed actual coefficient source.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Finite products of actual local spectra]
  \label{prop:actual-local-finite-products}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.isHF2Local_pi,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceHF2Shift_pi_isLocal,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.CompletedSpectrum.pi}

  \leanok
  Finite pointwise products of actual $H\mathbb F_2$-local prespectra are local. In particular, finite products of the specified integer coefficient shifts are local. These products and their projections satisfy the categorical product universal property in the full category of actual local spectra.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Use the proved ordinary-source product cone. Precomposition with an $H\mathbb F_2$ equivalence is a bijection into every factor, so it is a bijection into the product. The same universal property restricts to local objects.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

  Apply the proved limiting source product cone. Its universal property proves each identity by testing the specified projections.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

  Maps into a source zero object are unique, proving locality. The source zero-object and finite-product universal properties restrict to local objects, and the inclusion leaves morphisms unchanged.

  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.CompletedSpectrum.hasFiniteBiproducts,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.CompletedSpectrum.suspensionFunctor_additive,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.CompletedSpectrum.desuspensionFunctor_additive}
  \uses{prop:actual-loop-concatenation-laws,prop:ordinary-source-product-pairing,prop:bilinear-hom-groups,prop:actual-local-zero-and-inclusion,prop:actual-local-suspension-equivalence}
  \leanok
  The original ordinary stable category and its full category of actual $H\mathbb F_2$-local spectra are preadditive and have finite biproducts. Their addition is constructed from actual loop concatenation on all localized hom sets. The additive zero is definitionally the original zero morphism. The inclusion, loops, suspension and desuspension preserve the constructed addition.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Use the actual finite product to concatenate arbitrary morphisms into a loop object and the internal path homotopies to prove its group laws. The fully faithful loop equivalence transports these groups to all original hom sets. Precomposition and target naturality give bilinearity, which forces commutativity. Restrict to local objects and use the proved finite products to obtain biproducts and additivity of the stated functors.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual stable homotopy is additive and additively represented]
  \label{prop:actual-stable-functor-and-sphere-additivity}

  \uses{prop:actual-source-preadditive,prop:ordinary-source-product-pairing,prop:actual-source-additive-homotopy,prop:ordinary-source-sphere-representability}

  The original abelian stable homotopy functors preserve the specified finite source products and are additive for the constructed source hom groups. The original sphere representation is an additive equivalence $\pi_d X\cong[S^d,X]$, with its existing sphere universe unchanged.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Use the actual finite-product stable group equivalence to identify the mapped limiting cone. Preservation of finite products implies additivity. Apply this additivity to the universal sphere class and the existing natural representation equivalence.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

  The actual last-coordinate loop comparison is an additive equivalence $\pi_d(\Omega X)\cong\pi_{d+1}X$. It uses the original cubical representatives, coordinate convention and stabilization.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Last-coordinate uncurrying commutes with concatenation in any old cube coordinate. Move a pair of classes to a common cofinal stage and use the proved concatenation formula for stable addition.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberProjection_map_exact,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiber_stableHomotopy_longExact,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableHomotopy_exact_of_levelLoop_exact}

  $\pi_{d+1}X\to\pi_{d+1}Y\to\pi_dF(f)\to\pi_dX\to\pi_dY$
  is exact at all three middle terms. The construction and boundary are natural for strict commuting squares. A square whose two vertical maps are stable equivalences induces a stable equivalence on these actual fibers.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Construct the levels from based paths ending at the target basepoint, with pointwise bonds. Explicit cubical lifts, path-prefix transport and nullhomotopies prove the three levelwise exactness statements. Cofinality transfers them to the original stable quotients, and the additive loop comparison gives the boundary. The induced path maps prove naturality; the abelian five lemma proves invariance under stable equivalences.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Stable groups and actual fibers detect zero objects and equivalences]
  \label{prop:actual-fiber-zero-detection}

  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableEquivalence_iff_source_fiber_isZero}
  \uses{prop:actual-prespectrum-fiber-sequence,prop:actual-source-additive-homotopy,prop:ordinary-source-zero-object}
  \leanok
  An actual prespectrum is zero in the original ordinary stable localization exactly when all its original integer stable homotopy groups vanish. A strict map is a stable equivalence exactly when its actual homotopy fiber is such a zero object.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Zero-preserving stable homotopy functors detect vanishing on zero objects. Conversely, vanishing makes the strict map to the point prespectrum a stable equivalence. The proved fiber sequence then characterizes both injectivity and surjectivity of every stable map.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Finiteness and lower bounds for actual homotopy fibers]
  \label{prop:actual-fiber-finite-and-bounded-groups}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiber_stableHomotopy_subsingleton,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiber_stableHomotopy_vanishes_below,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiber_stableHomotopy_finite}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberProjection_stableHomotopy_finite_kernel}
  \uses{prop:actual-prespectrum-fiber-sequence}
  \leanok
  The group $\pi_dF(f)$ is finite, respectively zero, if $\pi_{d+1}Y$ and $\pi_dX$ are finite, respectively zero. If $X$ and $Y$ vanish below $a$ and $b$, then $F(f)$ vanishes below $\min(a,b-1)$. The kernel of $\pi_dF(f)\to\pi_dX$ is finite whenever $\pi_{d+1}Y$ is finite.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Use the actual boundary and projection exactness. Their kernel--image description gives the finite-group bound and the vanishing conclusion; the next target group surjects onto the stated kernel.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

  The actual pointwise pullback of two strict prespectrum maps has its specified projections, lift and strict categorical universal property. \end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Take the levelwise equalizer of the two maps out of the product. Strict bonding compatibility preserves its defining equation. The inherited topology gives the lift and its uniqueness. Apply pointwise postcomposition to paths, and use compact-open currying to assemble compatible paths into the pullback.

\end{proof}

  Every strict prespectrum map $f:X\to Y$ factors as $X\xrightarrow{i}P_f\xrightarrow{p}Y$, where $P_f$ retains a path starting at $f(x)$.   The actual mapping-path projection has a continuous strict lift of every strict prespectrum homotopy with compatible initial lift. The lift agrees exactly with that initial value, projects to the prescribed homotopy at every time and keeps the original source coordinate fixed.   Join the retained path and the prescribed base homotopy using the two regions $(1+s)t\le1$ and $(1+s)t\ge1$, with continuous clipped parameters. They agree on their closed intersection. The same formulas preserve basepoints and commute with every bond; evaluating them proves the exact endpoint and relative statements.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

A supplied actual lift of the universal retained null-path makes this comparison a stable equivalence, with explicit inverse and homotopies. A compatible pair of strict maps with a specified homotopy has a unique lift retaining all three. Its second projection has a constructed strict homotopy lifting, and its actual fiber is isomorphic in the original source to the actual fiber of $f$.   \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.StrictHomotopy,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.StrictHomotopy.levelPath,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.StrictHomotopy.refl}

  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.pathHomotopic_symm,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.pathHomotopic_trans,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.pathHomotopic_precomp}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.pathHomotopic_postcomp,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.pathHomotopic_congruence,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.source_map_eq_of_pathHomotopic}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableEquivalence_iff_of_pathHomotopic,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableEquivalences_hasHomotopyQuotient,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.HomotopyCategory}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.homotopyQuotient,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.homotopyToSource,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.homotopyStableEquivalences}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.homotopyQuotient_map_eq_iff,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.homotopyToSource_fac,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.homotopyToSource_map}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.homotopyStableEquivalences_map_iff,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.stableEquivalences_homotopy_inverseImage,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.homotopyQuotient_map_surjective}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.homotopyToSource_inverts,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.homotopyToSourceUniversalProperty,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.homotopyToSource_isLocalization}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.homotopyPullbackStrictHomotopy,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.homotopyPullbackDiagonal,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.homotopyPullbackDiagonal_fst}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.homotopyPullbackDiagonal_snd,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.homotopyPullbackDiagonal_stableEquivalence,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.pathHomotopic_cancel_stable}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.homotopyStableEquivalences_eq_inverseImage_isomorphisms,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.homotopyStableEquivalences_isMultiplicative,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.homotopyStableEquivalences_hasRightCalculusOfFractions}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.source_map_eq_iff_exists_homotopy_precomp}
  \uses{prop:actual-prespectrum-pullbacks-and-paths,prop:actual-homotopy-pullback-stable-equivalences}
  \leanok
  Strict based path homotopy is an actual congruence on prespectra, and equality in its quotient is exactly existence of such a homotopy. The original source is the localization of this actual homotopy category at the descended stable equivalences. Those equivalences admit right fractions on the quotient. In particular, two strict maps have equal images in the original source if and only if precomposition with some strict stable equivalence makes them strictly homotopic.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Construct reversal, concatenation and both composition operations on actual strict path homotopies. Factor the original localization through the proved congruence and verify its localization universal property. Actual homotopy pullbacks give the Ore square. A second homotopy pullback against the constant-path diagonal proves cancellation, yielding the right-fraction equality criterion.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Strict roofs and stable detection for all source morphisms]
  \label{prop:actual-source-roofs-and-conservativity}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceHasRoof,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceHasRoof_map,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceHasRoof_inverse}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceHasRoof_stableUnderComposition,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceHasRoof_all,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.source_morphism_exists_roof}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.source_isIso_iff_stableHomotopy_bijective}
  \uses{prop:actual-homotopy-pullback-stable-equivalences,prop:actual-source-homotopy-functor}
  \leanok
  Every morphism in the original ordinary stable localization has an actual strict span representative whose left leg is a stable equivalence. The original integer stable homotopy functors jointly detect isomorphisms of every localized morphism, including composites of formal inverses.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Strict maps and formal inverses have the indicated roofs. Actual homotopy pullback composes them, so generation of the localization by these maps gives every roof. If a localized map is bijective on every stable group, its roof numerator is a stable equivalence as well as its denominator, hence the map is invertible.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceFiberRotationIso_projection,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberLoopNullLevel,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberLoopNullPath}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberLoopNullPath_zero,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberLoopNullPath_one,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberRotationComparison_projection}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberRotationTargetMap_stableEquivalence,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberRotationComparison_target_retraction,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.source_fiberRotationComparison_target_retraction}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberRotationComparison_stableEquivalence,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceLoopAdd_eq_add,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceLoopNeg_eq_neg}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.source_loopInverse_eq_neg_id,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberIterationConnecting_retraction,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceFiberIterationIso_connecting}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.source_fiberRotationComparison_target_retraction_eq_neg}
  \uses{prop:actual-prespectrum-fiber-sequence,prop:actual-homotopy-quotient-fractions,prop:actual-loop-concatenation-laws,prop:actual-source-preadditive}
  \leanok
  The actual map $\Omega Y\to F(F(f)\to X)$ has a displayed strict homotopy inverse and is natural for strict squares. A second actual map $\Omega X\to F(\Omega Y\to F(f))$ is a stable equivalence, with projection exactly $\Omega f$. The original loop concatenation and reversal induce the original additive operations. Under the first fiber-iteration isomorphism the connecting map identifies with $-\Omega f$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Move the source point along its retained null-path and transport the target path by the reversed initial segment. Explicit padding homotopies give the two inverse identities. Compare the next fiber to the first iteration and remove its final constant path. The original loop group construction identifies path reversal with negation, fixing the rotation sign.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual fibers are exact on all represented source hom groups]
  \label{prop:actual-source-fiber-hom-exactness}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberLift_projection,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberLift_nullPath,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberHom_ext}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberLift,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.source_fiberProjection_exact,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.source_fiberProjection_hom_exact}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.source_fiberConnecting_exact,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.source_fiberConnecting_hom_exact,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.source_loopMap_fiberConnecting_exact}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.source_loopMap_fiberConnecting_hom_exact,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.source_fiber_hom_longExact,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.source_exists_fiber_sequence}
  \uses{prop:actual-prespectrum-fiber-sequence,prop:actual-homotopy-quotient-fractions,prop:actual-source-roofs-and-conservativity,prop:actual-source-preadditive,prop:actual-fiber-iteration-and-rotation}
  \leanok
  A strict map with an actual strict nullhomotopy of its composite has a unique strict lift to the actual fiber retaining that nullhomotopy. For every ordinary source object $A$ and strict map $f:X\to Y$, the additive sequence $[A,\Omega X]\to[A,\Omega Y]\to[A,F(f)]\to[A,X]\to[A,Y]$ is exact at all three middle terms. Every morphism of the original localization, including formal-inverse composites, admits such a fiber sequence exact on all represented hom groups.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Represent a localized kernel element by a strict roof and refine its zero composite to a strict nullhomotopy. Lift it to the actual fiber and invert the stable denominator. Apply this same lifting theorem to the fiber projection and connecting map, using the actual iteration and rotation isomorphisms. A roof for an arbitrary localized morphism transports the three exactness statements across its invertible denominator.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual fiber maps from homotopy-commuting squares]
  \label{prop:actual-homotopy-square-fiber-maps}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberHomotopyMap_projection,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberHomotopyMap_nullPath,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberConnecting_homotopyMap}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.source_fiberConnecting_homotopyMap,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberProjectionStrictNullhomotopy,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberSquareNullhomotopy}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberHomotopyMap,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberHomotopyMap_stableEquivalence,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.source_fiber_map_isIso}
  \uses{prop:actual-source-fiber-hom-exactness,prop:actual-homotopy-quotient-fractions,prop:actual-loop-concatenation-laws}
  \leanok
  A square equipped with an actual strict based homotopy induces an actual map of path fibers. It strictly commutes with the fiber projections and commutes with the connecting maps in the original source. If both vertical maps are stable equivalences, the induced fiber map is a stable equivalence. More generally, any source map between the actual fiber sequences compatible with invertible source and target comparisons is invertible.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Prepend the reversed square homotopy to the transported retained null-path. At the basepoint this adds only an initial constant loop, removed by the explicit unit homotopy. Apply the additive five lemma to the proved represented long exact sequences and then the Yoneda isomorphism criterion.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual fiber triangles and completion of source squares]
  \label{prop:actual-fiber-triangles-and-square-completion}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceSuspensionLoopCounitIso,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceFiberBoundary,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceFiberTriangle}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceFiberIterationSuspensionIso,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceFiberTriangleMap,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceFiberBoundary_natural}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceFiberIterationIso_inv_connecting,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceFiberIterationSuspensionIso_boundary,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceFiberIterationSuspensionIso_connecting}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceFiberTriangleRotateIso,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.source_square_exists_homotopy_roof,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.source_fiberMap_exists}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.source_fiberIso_exists,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceFiberTriangleMap_exists,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceFiberTriangle_morphism_exists}
  \uses{prop:actual-fiber-iteration-and-rotation,prop:actual-homotopy-square-fiber-maps,prop:actual-homotopy-quotient-fractions,prop:actual-source-roofs-and-conservativity,prop:actual-additive-integer-shifts}
  \leanok
  The actual fiber of a strict map defines a genuine triangle using the fixed suspension and the signed boundary $-\epsilon^{-1}\Sigma c_f$. Rotating the fiber triangle of its projection gives a specified isomorphism to the original fiber triangle. Every commuting source square has a common strict roof equipped with two actual square homotopies. Consequently it extends to a map of the actual fiber sequences, and an isomorphic square extends to a fiber isomorphism. Every commuting square on the first two, or on the second and third, objects of two actual fiber triangles extends to a genuine triangle morphism.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  The actual suspension-loop counit and the proved signed iteration formulas give all three squares of the rotation isomorphism. Choose roofs for the source square and take an actual homotopy pullback of their denominators. Refine the resulting localized equality to a strict homotopy, form the two actual fiber maps, and invert the stable comparison. The boundary naturality identity gives the triangle morphism. For the first-two-object version, apply this construction to the two fiber projections, rotate, and conjugate by the specified rotation isomorphisms.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual loop and suspension comparisons for fibers]
  \label{prop:actual-loop-fiber-comparison}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberLoopToLoopFiber_comp,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.loopFiberToFiberLoop_comp,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberLoopToLoopFiber_projection}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.loopFiberToFiberLoop_projection,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberLoopTransposedPath,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberLoopToLoopFiber}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.loopFiberSourcePath,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.loopFiberTransposedPath,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.loopFiberToFiberLoop}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberLoopIso,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceSuspensionLoopUnitIso,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.source_suspension_fiber_iso_exists}
  \uses{prop:actual-prespectrum-loops,prop:actual-fiber-triangles-and-square-completion,prop:actual-additive-integer-shifts}
  \leanok
  Transposing the two actual compact-open path parameters gives a strict prespectrum isomorphism $F(\Omega f)\cong\Omega F(f)$ preserving the fiber projection. The actual suspension-loop adjunction has an invertible unit in the original stable source. For every strict map $f$, there is an isomorphism $\Sigma F(f)\cong F(\Sigma f)$ in that source preserving the suspended fiber projection.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  The transpose and its inverse are explicit strict maps. Apply the fiber isomorphism theorem to the naturality square of the invertible adjunction unit, compose with the actual loop-fiber transpose, suspend, and use the actual counit. Naturality and the adjunction triangle identity prove the projection formula.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[The actual source is pretriangulated]
  \label{prop:actual-source-pretriangulated}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourcePretriangulated,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceFiberDistinguished,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceFiberDistinguished_fiber}

  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberZeroIso,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceFiberZeroTriangleIso,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.source_contractibleTriangle_iso_exists}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberZeroSection_projection,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.fiberZeroProjection_section,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.source_map_is_fiberProjection}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.source_morphism_is_fiberProjection,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.source_morphism_exists_fiber_triangle}
  \uses{prop:actual-loop-fiber-comparison,prop:actual-fiber-triangles-and-square-completion,prop:actual-source-roofs-and-conservativity,prop:ordinary-source-zero-object}
  \leanok
  In the original stable localization, let distinguished triangles be precisely those isomorphic to actual fiber triangles. With the already fixed integer shift this class defines an actual pretriangulated structure: it contains contractible triangles, completes every morphism as the first arrow of a triangle, is closed under both rotations and isomorphisms, and completes every commuting first-arrow square. Any two composable source maps can also be represented simultaneously by composable strict maps up to object isomorphisms.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  The fiber of the zero map to the actual zero prespectrum gives the contractible triangle. Suspend the fiber of the connecting map and use its projection-preserving comparisons to realize any strict map as a fiber projection; source roofs give arbitrary maps. The signed iteration isomorphism gives inverse rotation. For positive rotation, complete the second arrow to a triangle, inverse-rotate that triangle, and identify it with the original using the fiber isomorphism theorem. Conjugate the proved fiber square completion by triangle isomorphisms. Successive roofs simultaneously represent composable maps.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

  For composable strict maps $f:X\to Y$ and $g:Y\to Z$, the actual map $q:F(gf)\to F(g)$ has fiber strictly homotopy equivalent to $F(f)$. There is a specified source triangle $F(f)\to F(gf)\to F(g)\to\Sigma F(f)$, isomorphic to the actual fiber triangle of $q$, whose boundary is the projection $F(g)\to Y$ followed by the boundary of $f$. The two adjacent faces are genuine triangle morphisms.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Construct the comparison and its retraction using the retained null-path square. Explicit padding and moving-square homotopies prove the two inverse identities. The projection and connecting formulas identify the boundary and give all three squares of the triangle isomorphism and of the two face maps.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[The actual source satisfies the octahedron axiom]
  \label{prop:actual-source-triangulated}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceFiberDistinguished_composite,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceFiberCompositeOctahedron',KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceFiberCompositeOctahedronTriangleIso}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceFiberCompositeOctahedron,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sourceIsTriangulated}
  \uses{prop:actual-source-pretriangulated,prop:actual-composite-fiber-triangle}
  \leanok
  The original stable localization, with the fixed integer suspension and actual fiber distinguished triangles, is triangulated. For every composable pair the actual composite-fiber fourth triangle gives an octahedron with its specified boundary and face maps.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  The strict-pair fourth triangle supplies the inverse-oriented octahedron. Rotate its triangles three times and negate the middle-object comparison to obtain the usual signed octahedron. Simultaneous strict roofs represent any composable source pair, and the octahedron transports across their object and triangle isomorphisms.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[The actual HF2-local category is triangulated]
  \label{prop:actual-local-triangulated}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.SourceIsHF2Local,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.SourceIsHF2Local.of_iso,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.SourceIsHF2Local.suspension}

  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.CompletedSpectrum.pretriangulated,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.CompletedSpectrum.toSource_isTriangulated,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.CompletedSpectrum.isTriangulated}
  \uses{prop:actual-source-triangulated,prop:actual-source-fiber-hom-exactness,prop:actual-local-suspension-equivalence,prop:actual-additive-integer-shifts,prop:actual-local-zero-and-inclusion}
  \leanok
  Actual $H\mathbb F_2$-local source objects are closed under isomorphisms, loops, and actual retained-path fibers. In a distinguished source triangle, locality of the second and third objects implies locality of the first, and locality of the first two implies locality of the third. The already fixed category of local spectra, with its existing integer shifts, is triangulated; its original fully faithful inclusion into the stable source is triangulated.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  The actual suspension-loop comparison transfers locality to loops. Apply the additive five lemma to the represented fiber sequences with precomposition by a coefficient equivalence. The actual fiber description of distinguished triangles transfers this closure to arbitrary triangles; rotation gives cofiber closure. Pull back the distinguished class through the fixed fully faithful shift-preserving inclusion, using the proved local cocones. The source octahedron lifts through that inclusion.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[The original stable homotopy functors are homological]
  \label{prop:actual-stable-homotopy-homological}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceStableHomotopyAddFunctor_isHomological,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceStableHomotopyAddFunctor_exact,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceStableHomotopyAddFunctor_longExact}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.source_fiberTriangle_stableHomotopy_longExact}
  \uses{prop:actual-source-pretriangulated,prop:actual-prespectrum-fiber-sequence,prop:actual-source-additive-homotopy}
  \leanok
  In every universe and every integer degree, the original additive stable homotopy functor is homological for the actual distinguished source triangles. Its sequence on a distinguished triangle is exact at each of the three successive positions. On a strict fiber triangle, the first two arrows are the original stable maps of the fiber projection and the original map; the last arrow after the boundary is the original stable map of the suspended projection.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  The actual fiber long exact sequence gives exactness for standard fiber triangles. Transfer it along their defining triangle isomorphisms and use the additive homological-functor criterion. Rotation gives the other two positions. The explicit definition of the fixed suspension and its source functor identifies the last strict map.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

  \label{prop:actual-spectrification-step}

  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.Representative.fromSpectrificationStep,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.spectrificationStepUnit_natural,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.spectrificationStepUnit_coherence}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.Representative.fromSpectrificationStep_next,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.Representative.fromSpectrificationStep_unit,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.Representative.unit_fromSpectrificationStep}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.Representative.fromSpectrificationStep_relation,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.spectrificationStepUnit_stableEquivalence}
  \uses{prop:actual-prespectrum-loops,prop:prespectrum-cofinal-vanishing}
  \leanok
  There is an actual functor $R$ on strict prespectra with $(RX)_n=\Omega X_{n+1}$ and bonds $(p,t)\mapsto(s\mapsto\sigma_{n+1}(p(t),s))$. The original adjoint bonds form a strict natural unit $\eta_X:X\to RX$, with $R\eta_X=\eta_{RX}$ strictly. This unit is a stable equivalence for every actual prespectrum.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Compact-open evaluation and currying define the actual levels, bonds and maps. The bond formula makes the unit and its coherence strict. Uncurry a stable representative in its first loop coordinate; both composites with the unit are exactly the original stabilization operation. Descending these formulas through the original stable relation proves bijectivity in every integer degree.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Multiplication by two on the actual inverted sphere]
  \label{prop:actual-sphere-invert-two-scalar}

  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sphereInvertTwo_desuspension_two_id_isIso}
  \uses{prop:scalar-invertible-direct-limit,prop:actual-source-additive-homotopy,prop:actual-source-roofs-and-conservativity,prop:actual-additive-integer-shifts}
  \leanok
  The fixed doubled-bond sphere $S[1/2]$ has bijective multiplication by two on every original stable homotopy group. Multiplication by two on that actual source object is therefore an isomorphism. The same is true on every specified positive suspension and padded-desuspension iterate used by the definition of 2-completeness.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  The defining continuous double traversal of the suspension coordinate makes the actual stabilization cube the concatenation of the ordinary sphere stabilization cube with itself. Thus each group bond is the ordinary sphere bond followed by doubling. Apply actual direct-limit scalar invertibility and the proved joint conservativity of the original stable homotopy functors. Additivity of the specified suspension and desuspension functors preserves the scalar isomorphism.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sequentialCoconeLevel,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sequentialColimitDesc,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sequentialLevelDiagram}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sequentialColimitLevel,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sequentialColimitLevelInclusion,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sequentialColimitLevelInclusion_map}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sequentialColimitLevelInclusion_point,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sequentialColimitLevelInclusion_jointly_surjective,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sequentialColimitCocone_isColimit}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.prespectrumHasSequentialColimits}
  \uses{def:point-set-prespectrum}
  \leanok
  Every actual sequential diagram of strict prespectra has a sequential colimit with the actual topological colimit at each level. Its structure maps are continuous and satisfy the based endpoint identities. The stage inclusions and induced maps from arbitrary compatible cocones are strict prespectrum maps and satisfy the actual colimit universal property.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Take the original topological colimit in each level, with its stage-zero basepoint. Curry compatible structure maps into the compact-open path space, descend them through the level colimit, and uncurry. Joint surjectivity of the stage maps verifies the point and endpoint identities, strictness, and the universal property.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Stable homotopy of actual closed sequential prespectrum unions]
  \label{prop:actual-stable-homotopy-sequential-colimits}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sequentialStableHomotopyCocone,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sequentialColimit_levelHomotopy_jointly_surjective,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sequentialColimit_levelHomotopy_eq_eventually}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sequentialColimit_stableHomotopy_jointly_surjective,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sequentialColimit_stableHomotopy_eq_eventually,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sequentialStableHomotopyCocone_isColimit}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.sequentialColimitInclusion_zero_stableEquivalence}
  \uses{prop:actual-prespectrum-sequential-colimits,prop:actual-sequential-homotopy-colimit,prop:prespectrum-cofinal-vanishing}
  \leanok
  For an actual sequential diagram of strict prespectra whose level spaces are $T_1$ and whose level transition maps are closed embeddings, the original stable homotopy groups preserve its actual sequential colimit. Level and stable representatives come from finite stages, and equality is witnessed at a later finite stage. If every initial-to-stage map is a stable equivalence, the actual initial-to-colimit inclusion is a stable equivalence.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Factor the finite-dimensional loop and its entire based homotopy through a level stage. For a stable equality, first choose its finite stabilization stage and then factor that witnessing homotopy. These representative and equality criteria give the set-colimit universal property. Stagewise bijectivity then gives bijectivity of the actual initial inclusion in every stable degree.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

  Twice the actual identity morphism of the fixed Dold--Kan source coefficient spectrum is zero in the original stable localization. The same holds for each of its specified integer shifts. The actual paired coefficient realization maps by a stable equivalence to the pointwise product, and its simplicial addition, diagonal, and two inclusions define strict prespectrum maps.

  Naturality of the actual cone structure maps makes the simplicial addition and diagonal into strict prespectrum maps. \begin{proposition}[Actual HF2 locality implies 2-completeness]
  \label{prop:actual-hf2-local-two-completeness}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.hf2Equivalence_zero_of_two_id_isIso,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.SourceIsHF2Local.hom_subsingleton_of_two_id_isIso,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.Prespectrum.IsHF2Local.isTwoComplete}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.CompletionModel.replacement_isTwoComplete}
  \uses{prop:scalar-orthogonality,prop:actual-hf2-scalar-two,prop:actual-sphere-invert-two-scalar}
  \leanok
  Every actual $H\mathbb F_2$-local prespectrum is 2-complete in the original sense: there are no nonzero source maps to it from any specified positive or negative shift of the fixed doubled-bond sphere $S[1/2]$. More generally a local target has no nonzero maps from any source object whose doubling is invertible. Consequently every value of any supplied HF2 reflection is 2-complete.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Scalar orthogonality makes the map from such a source to zero an equivalence on every actual shifted HF2 cohomology group. The defining locality property then gives a bijection from the singleton maps out of zero. Apply the actual invertible doubling theorem for each of the specified sphere shifts.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

The construction of that fixed reflection is still unfinished, so this closed application still depends on the unfinished reflection existence theorem.
\end{proposition}

  Every strict prespectrum map $f:X\to Y$ has an actual reduced mapping cylinder $M(f)$ and strict maps $i:X\to M(f)$, $j:Y\to M(f)$, $q:M(f)\to Y$, with $qi=f$ and $qj=1$. A strict based strong deformation contracts $M(f)$ onto $Y$, fixing the entire target. The map $q$ induces bijections on all actual level homotopy sets and is a stable equivalence; if $f$ is a stable equivalence, so is $i$. When the input level spaces are $T_1$, the actual source inclusions are closed embeddings and the cylinder level spaces are $T_1$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Form the actual reduced pushout at each level and descend the compatible continuous structure maps. The maximum of the cylinder and homotopy parameters gives the strict deformation, which fixes the reduced basepoint and target. This proves the original level homotopy and stable equivalences. The quotient description identifies preimages of points and of the source end as closed sets, proving the stated separation and closed embedding properties.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual source limits of HF2-local objects]
  \label{prop:actual-local-source-limits}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.SourceIsHF2Local.of_isLimit,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.CompletedSpectrum.liftSourceLimit,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.CompletedSpectrum.toSource_createsLimit}

  \uses{prop:actual-local-zero-and-inclusion,prop:actual-local-triangulated}
  \leanok
  Every actual source limit of a diagram of HF2-local objects is HF2-local. The existing inclusion of completed spectra into the original source creates every such limit. Consequently source limits of any specified shape, once constructed, give limits of that shape in the existing completed category.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  For any HF2-equivalence, its maps into a local limit cone factor uniquely at each object. Uniqueness gives compatibility of those factors with every diagram arrow, and the actual limit universal property gives their unique joint factor. Equip the identical source cone point with this proved locality; full faithfulness reflects the limit property.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

Each step and every initial-to-stage map is a stable equivalence. The actual stable homotopy theorem for closed sequential unions proves that the displayed telescope unit is a stable equivalence.

\end{proof}

\end{proposition}

  Curry the first cubical coordinate to construct an actual inverse one stage later in the reduced mapping cylinder. The two composites agree up to the displayed strong deformation with the actual transition maps. Factor loops and witnessing homotopies through finite stages of the closed telescope to descend the inverse and prove both identities.

The displayed colimit has the actual based-space colimit at every level, with genuine continuous structure maps, strict inclusions, and its full universal property. Forgetting the point preserves connected based-space colimits, including pushouts and sequential colimits, so those level colimits are also the actual topological colimits.

The based-space colimit universal property proves strictness, factorization, and uniqueness for any cocone.   Bijective stabilization identifies every original stable group with its initial representative stage.   The actual quotient of each target level by the image of a strict prespectrum map forms a strict point-set cofiber, with its quotient map and canonical map from the literal mapping cone. If the original map has the actual homotopy-extension property at every level, that comparison is a stable equivalence, witnessed by based homotopy inverses at each level. The literal cone of an identity also has an actual strict contraction.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}

The two retained homotopies prove both inverse identities up to based homotopy. Homotopy invariance gives bijections on every level homotopy set and thus a stable equivalence. Multiplication and contraction of the two cone parameters give the identity-cone contraction.

\end{proof}

  The original stable localization has categorical products indexed by every small type, including countable products. The actual product functor is right adjoint to the diagonal, and its displayed cone satisfies the limit universal property. Both the original set-valued and abelian-group-valued stable homotopy functors preserve every such small product.

  Actual cubical replacement identifies the localization of T1 prespectra with the original source. Actual spectrification then identifies it with the localization of stabilized T1 prespectra. The same two resolutions work on arbitrary families. Pointwise products of stabilized objects preserve the actual stable equivalences, so localize their diagonal--product adjunction using the proved family localization. Its counit gives the required limiting cone. Compare it with the pointwise cone on stabilized inputs and use the actual stable-group product bijection to prove preservation of products.

\end{proof}

A strict pushout square induces a specified strict isomorphism between the corresponding quotient cofibers. Smashing with a based space commutes with the actual quotient cofiber when the target levels are compactly generated. This comparison preserves quotient projections and commutes with the canonical map from the literal cone.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Maps out of the quotient are precisely strict target maps vanishing on the original image. The actual pushout universal property identifies those maps on its two sides and constructs inverse quotient maps. The smash comparison is defined on representatives and checked against the collapse relations; the quotient formulas prove its inverse and all projection and cone compatibility identities.
  \leanok
\end{proof}
