Every actual inverse sequence, in either the original stable category or this local category, has a constructed Milnor homotopy limit with its specified distinguished triangle for $1-\mathrm{shift}$. The common inverse-sequence and Milnor language requires only its categorical, additive and pretriangulated structures, with no tensor premise. This provides the chosen homotopy-limit objects and their actual witnesses in both constructed categories. The finite-group difference theorem supplies this surjectivity when the stated represented groups are finite.

  Transport homotopy extension across the actual topological pushout using its universal property. Evaluation and the connected based forgetful functor preserve the strict pushout. \end{proof}

  In the fixed original source category, the original stable homotopy group of the constructed Milnor limit maps surjectively to the compatible families of stage classes.   The original sphere representation identifies compatible stable classes with compatible represented maps. The actual sphere-shift isomorphism identifies their next degree with the shift required by the proved Milnor uniqueness theorem. Finite-group or surjective-transition difference equations kill precisely the boundary ambiguity. The original additive representation retains the actual addition, giving the stated additive equivalence. Apply the two-degree comparison below the stated bound.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\end{proof}

  Traverse the outer cone backwards and then the inner cone to construct the inverse. \begin{proposition}[Scalar triangles and represented vanishing]
  \label{prop:scalar-triangle-and-represented-detection}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.postcomp_nsmul_id_bijective,KIP126.StableHomotopy.isIso_nsmul_pow_id,KIP126.StableHomotopy.isIso_nsmul_id_shift}
  \lean{KIP126.Foundation.nsmul_id_square_eq_zero_of_scalar_triangle,KIP126.Foundation.postcomp_shift_injective_of_desuspension,KIP126.Foundation.hom_triangle_third_subsingleton_of_postcomp}
  \uses{def:mathlib-pretriangulated}
  \leanok
  In an additive category, scalar invertibility makes scalar postcomposition
  bijective and is retained by scalar powers and additive shifts.
  In any pretriangulated category with additive integer shifts, the third vertex of a distinguished triangle for $n\operatorname{id}$ is annihilated by $n^2$. Desuspension transports injectivity of represented postcomposition to the shifted target map. In a distinguished triangle, surjectivity in the original represented degree and injectivity in the desuspended degree imply vanishing of maps to the third vertex.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Triangle exactness factors the scalar endomorphism through the boundary, and its second application vanishes. Naturality of the actual shift cancellation transports injectivity. The two exactness steps then kill the boundary of every represented third-vertex map and its remaining lift.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

  The original source category has chosen cofiber triangles and completions of commuting squares, obtained from its proved triangle axioms. Their scalar-power cofiber tower has an actual Milnor limit and a chosen unit with every prescribed projection. Each tower stage is annihilated by the square of its defining scalar. The limit is right orthogonal to every object with invertible scalar action and all its integer shifts. At $p=2$, it satisfies the original point-set two-completeness predicate.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Choose the third vertices and square completions supplied by the actual source triangle axioms. Lift the compatible cofiber inclusions through the constructed Milnor triangle. Scalar annihilation makes all maps from scalar-invertible objects to a stage vanish. Both consecutive represented degrees therefore vanish, and the proved Milnor uniqueness theorem gives limit orthogonality. Apply this to the actual shifts of the doubled-bond sphere.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

In particular, the actual Moore completion of the original sphere is connective, for every natural scalar $p$. No finiteness hypothesis on the group in degree $N$ is needed.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  The actual cofiber triangles give the stage vanishing and surjectivity of the cofiber inclusions in the boundary degree. Compatibility of the inclusions makes the transition maps surjective there. Apply the proved surjective-transition Milnor lower-bound criterion and the original sphere connectivity theorem.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Universal property of the actual source Moore unit]
  \label{prop:actual-source-moore-universal}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceMooreCompletion_hom_ext_of_torsion,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceMooreCompletionUnit_postcomp_bijective_of_torsion,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.sourceMooreCompletionUnit_cofiber_scalar_isIso}

  \leanok
  For every bounded scalar-torsion source object $U$, the actual Moore unit induces a bijection on maps out of $U$. Its actual source cofiber has invertible scalar action. Every map to an object right orthogonal to all scalar-invertible source objects extends uniquely across that unit. If the original object is already so orthogonal, the specified unit is an isomorphism.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  The uniform torsion exponent makes the Moore tower's represented difference map surjective and identifies its compatible families with maps to the original object. The Milnor comparison therefore gives the torsion bijection. Test the unit on shifted scalar cofibers and apply the two-degree exactness criterion to prove scalar invertibility on its cofiber. Exactness gives unique extension; apply it twice to obtain the inverse unit for an already orthogonal object.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual Moore units and coefficient equivalences]
  \label{prop:actual-source-moore-coefficient-equivalence}

  \leanok
  For every original source object, its actual dyadic Moore unit is an HF2 equivalence in the original all-shift cohomological sense. For an already HF2-local object, this unit is an isomorphism, and its Moore completion is HF2-local.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  The original coefficient shifts are annihilated by two, so they are orthogonal to every two-invertible object. Apply the actual Moore universal property to each coefficient shift. The proved orthogonality of an HF2-local object to all two-invertible objects makes its own unit an isomorphism, and locality transports across this actual isomorphism.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Actual additive idempotent Moore functor]

  The same actual Moore object assignment extends to an additive endofunctor of the original source category, with the same specified units forming a natural transformation. The unit at a completed object is invertible and agrees with completion of the original unit. Consequently the twice-applied functor has a specified natural isomorphism to the once-applied functor.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Extend each composite with the target unit uniquely across the source unit. The proved unique-extension property gives identity, composition, zero and addition laws, and unit naturality. The completed object is already orthogonal, so its unit is invertible. Uniqueness identifies both unit maps and assembles the inverse units into the natural idempotence isomorphism.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

  \leanok
  The original source condition of orthogonality to all scalar-invertible objects is preserved by actual isomorphisms, all integer shifts and all three positions of distinguished triangles. Every actual Moore object satisfies this condition. Shifting the actual Moore unit retains its universal property, yielding a specified natural isomorphism between completion after any integer shift and that shift after completion. These comparisons satisfy the zero and addition coherence laws, and the original unit commutes with these shifts.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Use the actual inverse-shift equivalence and scalar invertibility of additive shifts to transport orthogonality and unique extension. Exactness of represented triangle maps gives the middle-vertex closure; rotations and shifted orthogonality give the other two positions. The two universal properties construct mutually inverse comparison maps, and uniqueness proves their naturality, zero and addition laws, and compatibility of the original unit.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Full relative cubical lifting for the actual mapping path]

  \leanok
  For every continuous map, projection from its actual mapping-path space to the endpoint has full relative cubical homotopy lifting. In particular the projection of the actual mapping-path prespectrum has this property at each level. The lift retains every prescribed horn value, including its entire existing path.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Use the actual cube-horn retraction to extend the source coordinate. The prescribed old paths and their initial and terminal values form a horn in one higher-dimensional cube. Apply the actual horn retraction there, then curry the resulting continuous path family. Its strict boundary equations verify the complete relative lifting statement.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

  \label{prop:actual-moore-exact-functor}

\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Complete the map of the first two vertices induced by the actual units using the source triangle axiom. Orthogonal closure makes the actual cofiber of the completed first map scalar-complete. Represented triangle exactness gives the third-vertex bijection on maps into complete targets. Its unique extension then compares that cofiber isomorphically with completion of the original third vertex, with all three triangle maps matching. The constructed shift coherence identifies this distinguished triangle with the functor's actual image triangle.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

  object.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Extend a map using the coefficient unit, the inverse of the smashed
  map, and coefficient multiplication. The unit law also proves
  uniqueness. Shift coherence transports this argument to each integer
  degree.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Finite mod-two homology of the sphere]
  \label{prop:finite-mod2-sphere}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.finiteMod2Type_sphere}
  \uses{def:stable-context,def:hf2-homology}
  \leanok
  In a supplied stable monoidal category, the sphere has degreewise finite
  mod-two homology for every supplied mod-two Eilenberg--Mac Lane object.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  The right unitor identifies $H\otimes S$ with $H$. The zeroth homotopy
  group of $H$ is $\mathbb F_2$ and every other homotopy group is zero.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Boundedness and finite mod-two type under isomorphism]
  \label{prop:bounded-finite-type-iso}

  \uses{def:admissible-spectrum,def:hf2-homology}
  \leanok
  An actual spectrum isomorphism preserves boundedness below and
  degreewise finite mod-two homology.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Postcomposition with the inverse is injective on represented homotopy
  groups. Tensoring an isomorphism with $H$ gives the same argument for
  mod-two homology.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

% SOURCE: MainPaper/main.tex:929-964 and all uses of distinguished triangles.
\begin{proposition}[Boundedness and finite type under shifts and cofibers]
  \label{prop:bounded-finite-type-closure}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.BoundedBelow.shift,KIP126.StableHomotopy.BoundedBelow.of_cofiber,KIP126.StableHomotopy.FiniteMod2Type.shift,KIP126.StableHomotopy.FiniteMod2Type.of_cofiber}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.BoundedBelow.of_cofiber_left,KIP126.StableHomotopy.BoundedBelow.of_cofiber_middle,KIP126.StableHomotopy.FiniteMod2Type.of_cofiber_left,KIP126.StableHomotopy.FiniteMod2Type.of_cofiber_middle}
  \uses{def:admissible-spectrum,def:hf2-homology,def:cofiber-triangle,def:stable-sphere-suspension}
  \leanok
  Every integer shift preserves boundedness below, and a cofiber triangle
  with bounded-below first two objects has bounded-below third object.
  Shift-compatible tensoring with $H$ preserves finite mod-two type under
  shifts; exact tensoring also preserves finite type under cofibers.
  In each case any two objects in a cofiber triangle having the property
  force the third to have it, using the rotated long exact sequence.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  A shift reindexes represented homotopy groups. The long exact sequence
  bounds the third object's homotopy groups below and expresses its
  mod-two homology as an extension of a quotient and a subgroup of finite
  groups.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

% SOURCE: MainPaper/main.tex:929-964 and all uses of distinguished triangles.
\begin{definition}[Cofiber and distinguished triangle data]
  \label{def:cofiber-triangle}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.HasFunctorialCofiber,KIP126.StableHomotopy.HoCofiberSequence,KIP126.StableHomotopy.HoCofiberSequence.ofMorphism,KIP126.StableHomotopy.FunctorialCofiberCoherence,KIP126.StableHomotopy.cofiberFactorizationMap_isIso}
  \uses{def:stable-context}
  \notready
  In an additive pretriangulated category, for every map $f:X\to Y$, choose a cofiber $C(f)$ and a distinguished
  triangle
  \[
    X\xrightarrow fY\longrightarrow C(f)\longrightarrow\Sigma X.
  \]
  The chosen square completions do not impose identity or composition laws.
  A morphism of triangles is three compatible maps, and a triangle equivalence
  is a morphism whose three components are isomorphisms.  Rotations and
  suspensions preserve distinguished triangles.  Every distinguished triangle
  whose first map is $f$ also carries a chosen triangle equivalence to this
  selected cofiber triangle; transporting along that equivalence is explicit
  data rather than definitional equality of its third object with $C(f)$.
  Lean has the chosen-cofiber and distinguished-triangle interfaces and
  invertibility of the cofiber comparison for a factorization through an
  isomorphism. The
  complete fixed cofiber construction and its coherent model comparisons
  are still required.
\end{definition}

% SOURCE: MainPaper/main.tex:253-271, 648-664, and 929-971; exactness used repeatedly.
\begin{theorem}[Stable homotopy long exact sequence]
  \label{thm:stable-homotopy-long-exact}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.connectingHomomorphism,KIP126.StableHomotopy.connectingHomomorphism_eq_desuspend,KIP126.StableHomotopy.les_homotopy_exact_f,KIP126.StableHomotopy.les_homotopy_exact_g,KIP126.StableHomotopy.lesHomotopyExactH,KIP126.StableHomotopy.connectingHomomorphism_naturality}
  \uses{def:stable-homotopy-group,def:cofiber-triangle,def:mathlib-homological-functor,thm:mathlib-homology-sequence-exact-one,thm:mathlib-homology-sequence-exact-two,thm:mathlib-homology-sequence-exact-three}
  \notready
  Every chosen cofiber triangle
  $X\to Y\to C(f)\to\Sigma X$ induces the natural long exact sequence
  \[
    \cdots\to\pi_{n+1}C(f)\to\pi_nX\to\pi_nY
      \to\pi_nC(f)\to\pi_{n-1}X\to\cdots,
  \]
  with connecting maps compatible with rotation, suspension, and morphisms of
  triangles.
  The three adjacent exactness statements and connecting-map naturality
  are already proved for a supplied abstract stable category. Application
  with all prescribed model, rotation, and suspension comparisons remains
  unfinished.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
  Equip the representable functor $[S^0,-]$ with Mathlib's homological-functor
  structure, apply its three homology-sequence exactness lemmas to every
  shift, and splice the rotated triangles.  Naturality follows from a morphism
  of triangles.
\end{proof}

% SOURCE: MainPaper/main.tex:1755-1921, smash diagrams in May's lemma and the Mahowald trick.
\begin{definition}[Smash of cofiber triangles]
  \label{def:smash-triangle}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.ClosedSymmetricTensorTriangulated,KIP126.StableHomotopy.HoCofiberSequence.map,KIP126.StableHomotopy.TensorTriangulatedCatWithFunctorialCofiber}
  \uses{def:cofiber-triangle}
  \notready
  Smashing a distinguished triangle with any spectrum gives a distinguished
  triangle, naturally in both variables.  For two cofiber triangles the
  induced $3\times3$ diagram contains the canonical pullback/pushout square
  and connecting maps used to compare the two ways of reaching the double
  cofiber.
  Exact-functor transport of a distinguished triangle and the selected
  tensor/cofiber comparison are implemented as abstract data. The full
  double-cofiber diagram and its prescribed fixed-model comparisons remain
  unfinished.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Finite-group Milnor comparison]
  \label{prop:finite-milnor-comparison}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.InverseSequence.postcomp_oneSubShift_surjective_of_finite,KIP126.StableHomotopy.SequentialHomotopyLimit.exists_lift,KIP126.StableHomotopy.SequentialHomotopyLimit.homotopy_ext_of_finite,KIP126.StableHomotopy.SequentialHomotopyLimit.existsUnique_homotopy_lift_of_finite}
  \uses{prop:finite-inverse-sequence-sub-shift,thm:stable-homotopy-long-exact}
  \leanok
  The represented difference and lift assertions require only a pretriangulated
  category with countable products. Fix an inverse sequence $D$ and a specified Milnor triangle exhibiting $L$ as its homotopy
  limit. Every compatible family of maps $W\to D_k$ lifts to a map $W\to L$.
  If every $\pi_{n+1}D_k$ is finite, a compatible family of classes in
  $\pi_nD_k$ has a unique lift in $\pi_nL$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Product projections identify compatibility with the kernel of
  $1-\mathrm{shift}$; exactness of the Milnor triangle gives existence.
  The finite-group result makes this map surjective on represented groups
  in degree $n+1$, so the connecting map vanishes and the lift is unique.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Constructed sequential homotopy limits]
  \label{prop:constructed-milnor-limit}
  \lean{KIP126.Core.InverseSequence.surjective_sub_shift_of_surjective}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.InverseSequence.homotopyLimit,KIP126.StableHomotopy.InverseSequence.homotopyLimitWitness,KIP126.StableHomotopy.InverseSequence.postcomp_oneSubShift_surjective_of_surjective}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.SequentialHomotopyLimit.homotopy_subsingleton_of_surjective,KIP126.StableHomotopy.SequentialHomotopyLimit.homotopy_subsingleton_of_finite,KIP126.StableHomotopy.SequentialHomotopyLimit.homotopy_subsingleton_of_uniform_bound,KIP126.StableHomotopy.InverseSequence.homotopyLimit_boundedBelow}
  \uses{prop:finite-milnor-comparison,def:cofiber-triangle}
  \leanok
  For an inverse sequence in a supplied stable category with countable
  products and chosen cofibers, $\Sigma^{-1}\operatorname{cofib}(1-\mathrm{shift})$
  has its specified Milnor triangle. If all stage homotopy groups in
  degree $n$ vanish, then the limit group in degree $n$ vanishes whenever
  the next-degree transition maps are surjective or all next-degree
  groups are finite. A uniform lower bound is retained when the boundary
  groups are finite.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Inverse rotation supplies the Milnor triangle. Surjective transitions
  solve the difference equation recursively; the finite-group version
  was proved above. Exactness eliminates the derived-limit term.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[The Milnor limit of an identity sequence]
  \label{prop:constant-milnor-limit}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.InverseSequence.constant,KIP126.StableHomotopy.InverseSequence.oneSubShift_epi_of_step_epi,KIP126.StableHomotopy.SequentialHomotopyLimit.hom_ext_of_step_epi,KIP126.StableHomotopy.SequentialHomotopyLimit.constant_π_eq,KIP126.StableHomotopy.SequentialHomotopyLimit.constant_isIso_of_projections,KIP126.StableHomotopy.SequentialHomotopyLimit.constant_exists_iso}
  \uses{prop:constructed-milnor-limit}
  \leanok
  The Milnor limit of the constant sequence on $X$ with identity
  transitions is isomorphic to $X$, with all projections identifying
  with the identity. More generally, epimorphic transition morphisms
  make the Milnor projections jointly monomorphic.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Epimorphisms split in a pretriangulated category. The resulting
  surjective represented transitions make $1-\mathrm{shift}$ an
  epimorphism, so its Milnor boundary vanishes. Exactness makes the
  projections jointly monomorphic. The diagonal lift for the constant
  sequence and its zeroth projection are inverse.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Represented detection and orthogonality for Milnor limits]
  \label{prop:milnor-represented-detection}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.SequentialHomotopyLimit.shiftedHom_ext_of_surjective,KIP126.StableHomotopy.SequentialHomotopyLimit.shiftedHom_subsingleton}
  \uses{prop:constructed-milnor-limit}
  \leanok
  Maps from $U[d]$ to a specified Milnor limit are determined by their
  stage projections when the difference operator on maps from $U[d+1]$
  to the stage product is surjective. If maps from both $U[d]$ and
  $U[d+1]$ to every stage vanish, maps from $U[d]$ to the limit vanish.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Apply the represented long exact sequence to the actual Milnor
  triangle. The next-degree surjectivity kills the ambiguity term.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Moore completion and its connectivity bound]
  \label{prop:moore-completion-connectivity}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.moorePowerMap,KIP126.StableHomotopy.moorePowerSequence,KIP126.StableHomotopy.mooreCompletion}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.moorePowerSequence_inclusion_step,KIP126.StableHomotopy.moorePowerSequence_homotopy_subsingleton,KIP126.StableHomotopy.moorePowerSequence_inclusion_surjective,KIP126.StableHomotopy.moorePowerSequence_step_surjective,KIP126.StableHomotopy.mooreCompletion_exists_unit,KIP126.StableHomotopy.mooreCompletion_homotopy_subsingleton,KIP126.StableHomotopy.mooreCompletion_boundedBelow}
  \uses{prop:constructed-milnor-limit}
  \leanok
  Construct $X/p^k$ using the chosen cofiber of $p^k\operatorname{id}_X$,
  with reduction maps induced by $(p\operatorname{id}_X,\operatorname{id}_X)$.
  Its constructed homotopy limit admits a unit with these actual cofiber
  projections. If $\pi_nX=0$ for $n<N$, its homotopy groups also vanish
  for $n<N$, with no finiteness assumption in degree $N$.
  Identification with the specified $H\mathbb F_2$ reflection is a further
  model theorem.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  The cofiber long exact sequence gives the levelwise vanishing and
  surjectivity of the reduction maps in degree $N$. The surjective
  Milnor criterion therefore removes the possible obstruction in degree
  $N-1$ as well. Compatibility of the actual inclusions gives the unit.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Orthogonality of Moore completion]
  \label{prop:moore-completion-orthogonality}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.moorePowerSequence_hom_subsingleton,KIP126.StableHomotopy.moorePowerSequence_shiftedHom_subsingleton,KIP126.StableHomotopy.mooreCompletion_shiftedHom_subsingleton}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.mooreCompletion_hom_subsingleton,KIP126.StableHomotopy.mooreCompletion_precomp_bijective}
  \uses{prop:moore-completion-connectivity,prop:milnor-represented-detection}
  \leanok
  If multiplication by $p$ is invertible on $U$, all maps
  $U[d]\to\widehat X_p$ vanish, in every integer degree. Consequently
  precomposition with a map whose cofiber has invertible multiplication
  by $p$ induces a bijection on maps to $\widehat X_p$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Scalar powers act bijectively on the represented source groups.
  The cofiber sequence makes maps to each Moore stage vanish, and
  the two-degree Milnor criterion gives the limit assertion.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Scalar residual limits and the Moore product triangle]
  \label{prop:scalar-residual-limit}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.scalarResidualSequence,KIP126.StableHomotopy.isIso_nsmul_id_cofiber_one_sub_nsmul,KIP126.StableHomotopy.scalarResidualSequence_oneSubShift}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.scalarResidualSequence_homotopyLimit_isIso_nsmul,KIP126.StableHomotopy.moorePowerTriangle,KIP126.StableHomotopy.moorePowerTriangleStep}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.mooreProductTriangle,KIP126.StableHomotopy.mooreProductTriangleDifference,KIP126.StableHomotopy.mooreProductTriangle_distinguished}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.mooreProductTriangleDifference_hom₁,KIP126.StableHomotopy.mooreProductTriangleDifference_hom₂,KIP126.StableHomotopy.mooreProductTriangleDifference_hom₃}
  \uses{prop:constant-milnor-limit,prop:moore-completion-connectivity}
  \leanok
  In an additive pretriangulated category with additive integer shifts,
  multiplication by $p$ is invertible on the cofiber of $1-pg$
  and on the homotopy limit of the constant-object sequence with
  transition $p\operatorname{id}$. Products of the actual Moore
  cofiber triangles form a distinguished triangle; the three components
  of its difference map are the scalar-residual, constant-identity,
  and Moore-sequence difference maps.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  In the cofiber long exact sequence, $1-pg$ gives a division
  formula for multiplication by $p$. Represented bijectivity detects
  invertibility. Products preserve distinguished triangles, and the
  defining projections verify the three component identities.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Moore units detected by bounded scalar torsion]
  \label{prop:moore-completion-torsion}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.nsmul_square_id_cofiber_eq_zero,KIP126.StableHomotopy.moorePowerSequence_iterate_mem_inclusion,KIP126.StableHomotopy.moorePowerSequence_sub_shift_surjective_of_torsion}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.moorePowerSequence_existsUnique_compatible_of_torsion,KIP126.StableHomotopy.mooreCompletion_unit_postcomp_bijective_of_torsion}
  \uses{prop:moore-completion-connectivity,prop:uniform-image-derived-limit,prop:milnor-represented-detection}
  \leanok
  The scalar and tower lemmas use only an additive pretriangulated category
  with chosen cofibers and the specified products.
  If $p^N\operatorname{id}_U=0$ and $u:X\to\widehat X_p$
  has the specified Moore projections, postcomposition by $u$ induces
  a bijection $[U,X]\to[U,\widehat X_p]$. The cofiber of
  $p\operatorname{id}$ is killed by $p^2$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  After $N$ reduction maps, every represented Moore class lies in
  the image of $[U,X]$. The uniform-image difference lemma eliminates
  the derived-limit term, and the cofiber exact sequence identifies
  compatible families with $[U,X]$.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[The universal property of the constructed Moore completion]
  \label{prop:moore-completion-universal}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.mooreCompletion_unit_cofiber_scalar_isIso,KIP126.StableHomotopy.mooreCompletion_unit_precomp_bijective,KIP126.StableHomotopy.mooreCompletion_exists_universal_unit}
  \uses{prop:moore-completion-orthogonality,prop:moore-completion-torsion}
  \leanok
  The scalar-cofiber detection and orthogonal extension assertions require
  only an additive pretriangulated category with chosen cofibers.
  The constructed Moore completion has a unit with all specified
  projections. Its cofiber has invertible multiplication by $p$.
  Every map from $X$ to an object right orthogonal to all
  $p$-invertible objects extends uniquely across this unit.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Test the unit on shifted scalar cofibers, which are killed by
  $p^2$. The torsion bijections and the long exact sequence show
  that its cofiber has no such represented maps, hence $p$ is
  invertible there. Orthogonality and exactness give unique extension.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Coefficient smash of a two-invertible object]
  \label{prop:coefficient-two-invertible-vanishing}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Cohomology.mod2_id_two_nsmul_zero,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Cohomology.mod2Free_isZero_of_two_isIso,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Cohomology.isZero_of_two_isIso_of_hf2_conservative}
  \uses{def:unital-multiplication-data,prop:coefficient-equivalence-cohomology}
  \leanok
  For the supplied mod-two ring object, $2\operatorname{id}_{H\mathbb F_2}=0$.
  Smashing an object on which $2$ is invertible with $H\mathbb F_2$
  gives zero. If this smash functor is conservative, that object
  itself is zero.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  The coefficient unit and multiplication give the scalar identity.
  Additivity makes its smash simultaneously invertible and zero;
  conservativity then reflects the zero-object criterion.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Moore completeness in the selected local category]
  \label{prop:fixed-moore-completeness}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.SourceComparison.isZero_of_two_isIso,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.SourceComparison.mooreCompletion_unit_isIso,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Implementation.SourceComparison.mooreCompletion_exists_iso}
  \lean{KIP126.Def.fixed_mooreCompletion_unit_isIso,KIP126.Def.fixed_mooreCompletion_exists_iso}
  \uses{prop:source-coefficient-positive-shifts,prop:coefficient-two-invertible-vanishing,prop:moore-completion-universal}
  \notready
  Every object of the selected realization is isomorphic to its
  constructed Moore $2$-completion, through a unit with the actual
  cofiber projections. This applies in particular to the fixed sphere,
  $C\nu$, and the selected $tmf$. The implemented proof makes the unit
  cofiber zero by source coefficient conservativity. Its dependencies
  retain the unfinished source and fixed-model constructions; Adams
  nilpotent completeness is a further assertion.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Product comparison through an actual coefficient approximation]
  \label{prop:tensor-product-comparison-approximation}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.tensor_piComparison_postcomp_bijective_of_approximation}
  \uses{def:mathlib-monoidal-category}
  \leanok
  Given $f:E\to H$, a family $X_j$, and a test object $W$,
  suppose $E\otimes\prod_jX_j\to\prod_j(E\otimes X_j)$ is
  invertible. If $[W,f\otimes\prod_jX_j]$ is surjective and
  every $[W,f\otimes X_j]$ is bijective, then the actual $H$
  product comparison is bijective on maps from $W$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  The tensor exchange law and product projections give a commuting
  square. The right side is bijective by the product universal property;
  surjectivity of the left side and bijectivity of the upper side give
  both claims for the lower comparison.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Transport of residual-tower acyclicity]
  \label{prop:inverse-sequence-acyclic-transport}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.InverseSequence.map,KIP126.StableHomotopy.InverseSequence.oneSubShift_conjugate,KIP126.StableHomotopy.InverseSequence.isAcyclic_iff_of_iso,KIP126.StableHomotopy.InverseSequence.map_oneSubShift,KIP126.StableHomotopy.InverseSequence.IsAcyclic.map,KIP126.StableHomotopy.InverseSequence.map_isAcyclic_iff,KIP126.StableHomotopy.InverseSequence.shift_isAcyclic_iff,KIP126.StableHomotopy.InverseSequence.IsAcyclic.bijective_sub_shift}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.InverseSequence.isAcyclic_of_step_eq_zero,KIP126.StableHomotopy.InverseSequence.isAcyclic_of_map_step_eq_zero}
  \uses{def:stable-context}
  \leanok
  A commuting family of stage isomorphisms preserves and reflects
  invertibility of the actual Milnor map $1-\mathrm{shift}$, using only the
  pretriangulated structures for these sequence results. An additive
  functor preserving the product of the specified stages preserves this condition; if it
  reflects isomorphisms, it also reflects the condition. In particular
  every integer shift preserves and reflects acyclicity. Representing an
  acyclic sequence by $[W,-]$ gives a bijective difference operator on
  the product of its represented groups.
  In particular, a conservative additive functor that commutes with
  this product and kills every transition detects acyclicity.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  Conjugate by the product of the stage isomorphisms, or by the canonical
  product comparison of the functor. Product projections identify the
  represented difference operator with postcomposition by the Milnor map.
  \leanok
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Finite homology from actual Milnor coordinates]
  \label{prop:hf2-finite-mod2-coordinates}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Cohomology.finiteMod2Type_hf2,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Cohomology.finiteMod2Type_of_homology_injective}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Cohomology.mod2Cooperations_negative_subsingleton}
  \uses{prop:h6-full-milnor-coordinates,def:h6-full-milnor-monomials,def:hf2-homology}
  \leanok
  For supplied reduced Milnor cooperation coordinates, $H\mathbb F_2$
  has finite mod-two homology in every integer degree. Any actual map
  $X\to H\mathbb F_2$ that is injective on each mod-two homology group
  therefore gives finite mod-two type of $X$.
  The same coordinates show that cooperations vanish in negative degrees.
\end{proposition}

\subsection{Homotopy classes and secondary operations}

% SOURCE: MainPaper/main.tex:2387-2616, Toda-bracket arguments.
\begin{definition}[Toda bracket]
  \label{def:toda-bracket}
  \lean{KIP126.StableHomotopy.Toda.Relation,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Toda.indeterminacy_complete,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Toda.relation_iff_indeterminacy,KIP126.StableHomotopy.Toda.juggling}
  \uses{def:cofiber-triangle}
  \notready
  Given composable stable maps $a,b,c$ with chosen nullhomotopies of $ba$ and
  $cb$, the triple Toda bracket $\langle c,b,a\rangle$ is a coset of homotopy
  classes with its standard left and right indeterminacy.  The interface
  includes suspension, naturality, containment under changed nullhomotopies,
  and Moss-style comparison with Massey products when an Adams convergence
  theorem supplies that comparison.
  The cone-based relation and its full additive coset and juggling laws
  are implemented, as are naturality, suspension and exact tensor-functor
  transport. The concrete named bracket computations and Moss comparisons
  required here remain separate unfinished obligations.
\end{definition}

% SOURCE: MainPaper/main.tex:2470-2567, Massey products on Adams pages; Moss Theorem 1.2.
\begin{definition}[Massey product]
  \label{def:massey-product}
  \lean{KIP126.Classical.Adams.Moss.PageMassey.Relation,KIP126.Classical.Adams.Moss.PageMassey.Indeterminacy}
  \uses{def:multiplicative-ss,def:mathlib-f2}
  \notready
  On a differential graded page model for the multiplicative Adams spectral
  sequence, composable classes $a,b,c$ with $ab=0$ and $bc=0$ and chosen
  nullhomologies define a triple Massey-product coset
  $\langle a,b,c\rangle$.  The interface records its bidegree, left and right
  indeterminacy, naturality, change-of-choice formula, and a proposition that
  the indeterminacy is zero in a specified finite bidegree.
  Lean implements the preceding-page defining-system relation and its
  additive indeterminacy for $E_r$ with $r\ge3$. This does not yet supply
  the separate $E_2$ cobar operation or the complete choice and naturality
  laws stated in this node.
\end{definition}
